Catalog/WAL/SHM, library-map, and config hashes, sizes, mtimes, logical counts, and policy values were identical before and after.

### INC-017: Legacy Git-tracked archive files lack restore-hash evidence
- date: 2026-07-16 / status: resolved; PR #23 merged and real repair/restore acceptance passed 2026-07-17 / triggered_by: RFC-001 Phase-G real-restore preflight / related: DEC-003, DEC-019, DEC-037, DEC-042, RFC-001 / docs_updated: modelark/archive_hash.py, modelark/fetch.py, modelark/hash_repair.py, modelark/restore.py, modelark/verifier.py, modelark/compress.py, modelark/cli.py, modelark/core/schema.sql, tests/test_hash_repair.py, README.md, docs/rfcs/001-migrated-runtime-acceptance.md, docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: Seven physically complete restore candidates would fail closed on files with no expected sha256. Every affected repository had a hashless `.gitattributes`; one also had three nested hashless evaluation YAML files. No restore or migrated-catalog mutation ran after discovery.
- root_cause: Legacy fetch computed and recorded `archived.orig_sha256` only when Hugging Face supplied `files.sha256`. Ordinary Git-tracked blobs commonly have no Hub digest and, because they are not annexed, no SHA256 annex-key fallback. Restore therefore had physical bytes but no independent expected digest with which to verify them.
- remediation: Every new download is now sha256-hashed before storage; a Hub-provided digest remains authoritative and must match, while the computed original-byte digest is always persisted and drives the compression canary. `repair-hashes` is read-only by default. Explicit apply accepts only raw regular files whose current bytes are identical to the blob at the same path in the archive's `HEAD`, refuses any unresolved/conflicting candidate as a whole, creates a consistent non-overwriting SQLite backup, revalidates after backup, and never overwrites existing evidence.
- safety_boundary: Git proves that repaired bytes match the archive's committed ingestion record, not that Hugging Face independently published that sha256. Restore continues to prove equality with the archived original bytes, and ingestion separately enforces the Hub digest whenever one exists. The real migrated-catalog repair and restore require operator approval after review; this incident does not authorize either action or Fill.
- review_update_2026_07_16: PR #23 merged after the Python 3.10/3.12 and Playwright CI matrix passed, and Greptile scored the final revision 5/5. The reviewed implementation unblocks installation and a read-only real-catalog audit; it does not itself approve `repair-hashes --apply`, restore output creation, or Fill.
- acceptance_update_2026_07_17: After separate operator approvals, 194/194 proposed Git-proven digests were applied backup-first and the repeated audit found no unresolved gap across 2,311 archived file rows. A disposable 12-file, 91,571,868-byte compressed restore reconstructed nested paths and matched every original-byte hash. The operator reviewed the evidence before cleanup; the consistent catalog backup remains available and valid.

### DEF-027: Defer first-class acquisition for unsupported-only artifact repositories
- date: 2026-07-15 / status: active / triggered_by: DEC-045 copied-catalog replay found four selected repositories with no safetensors, GGUF, or opted-in pickle manifest / related: DEC-036, DEC-037, DEC-040, DEC-045, INC-015, RFC-001 / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Keep unsupported-only repositories selected and visibly blocked rather than silently producing auxiliary-only archives or guessing how to restore them. A format becomes supported only with one canonical manifest rule, storage action, relative-path preservation, verification evidence, restore coverage, capacity budgeting, and mixed-cart tests.
- revisit_when: the operator wants one of the four parked repositories in the active fill, or a generally useful artifact format can satisfy the full archive/restore contract.

### DEF-028: Defer partial continuation past a structurally infeasible Gate-B requirement
- date: 2026-07-15 / status: active / triggered_by: DEC-045 completion-contract review / related: DEC-019, DEC-031, DEC-045, INC-014, INC-015, RFC-001 / docs_updated: docs/plans/reconciled-work-graph-capacity-ledger.md, docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Retain whole-plan Gate-B admission for this release. Do not automatically run a feasible subset when another committed requirement is structurally blocked. A future explicit partial mode must define subset selection, operator consent, persistent/resume semantics, loud unmet-requirement UI, and how completion is reported without implying the whole plan was fulfilled.
- revisit_when: after RFC-001 acceptance and a real restored/fill cycle prove the reconciled whole-plan contract, or when a recurring blocker makes operator-chosen partial progress materially valuable.

### RFC-001: Operator-attended migrated-runtime acceptance
- date: 2026-07-15 / status: in execution; Phases A–G passed, Phase H stopped safely pending issue #28 / triggered_by: DEC-042 live migration + operator request for a public, checklist-driven final test / related: DEC-024, DEC-035, DEC-037, DEC-038, DEC-040, DEC-042, DEC-044, DEC-045, DEC-046, DEF-011, DEF-027, DEF-028, DEF-031, INC-014, INC-015, INC-016, INC-017, INC-018, INC-019 / standalone: `docs/rfcs/001-migrated-runtime-acceptance.md`
- proposal: Validate the canonical installed CLI, schema-v2 migrated catalog, durable plan membership, reconciled graph/ledger, and loopback portal in explicit phases. Treat plan membership and current mount/write availability as separate facts: selection and planning may use the complete registered fleet while shelved or read-only drives remain unavailable to execution. The first attended session starts no fill and stops automation once the non-resuming portal is ready for the operator to choose the cart.
- gates: Preserve the stopped legacy catalog plus raw, snapshot, SQL-dump, and migration-manifest rollback evidence. Run CLI planning without `--apply`; start the portal without `--resume`; never select models or start fill for the operator. A real restore, service installation, and fill resume are later explicit continuations. A new drive is not a testing prerequisite; genuine post-selection infeasibility is resolved by an operator-approved cart or capacity change.
- publication_boundary: The RFC and sanitized checklist are public-build artifacts. Runtime evidence must omit private workstation paths, usernames, drive identifiers, network addresses, and credentials. During the attended acceptance session, Git is read-only unless the operator separately authorizes publication actions.
- execution_update_2026_07_15: Phases A–C passed. Phase D stopped before portal launch: installed CLI help and plan list/show passed, and the reconciled `library plan --explain` returned exact tasks/ledgers plus typed blocking diagnostics, but the legacy-compatible `library plan --json` raised the first selected repository's `ArchivePolicyError`. The portal Fill plan and queue endpoints call the same legacy projection, so Phase E was not started. Acceptance requires a reviewed adapter to the reconciled result that preserves typed blockers and the existing UI contract; no unreviewed runtime patch was installed during the Git-read-only session.
- remediation_candidate_2026_07_15: A local, unpromoted adapter now publishes the canonical graph/ledger through the existing CLI and portal shapes, keeps blocked repositories visible, exposes typed diagnostics, and disables Fill admission. Mixed-cart unit/CLI/HTTP/browser coverage and the 210-test suite pass; read-only replay retains 444/444 queue rows and all 54 manifest-policy blockers with no capacity failures. RFC-001 remains stopped until review, public publication, canonical reinstall, and Phase-D repetition.
- publication_update_2026_07_15: The adapter merged as PR #16 with green Python 3.10/3.12 and Playwright CI. Greptile's post-merge 4/5 pass confirmed the core safety behavior and identified three bounded projection defects now tracked by `INC-015`. RFC-001 remains stopped until the follow-up is reviewed, public `main` is installed into the hidden canonical tree, and Phase D is repeated from that installed build.
- installed_quality_update_2026_07_16: PR #17's projection corrections and PR #18's wheel-mode test contract were installed from reviewed public `main`. The exact direct-file suite against site-packages, full 212-test pytest suite, Ruff, fresh-wheel resource/migration smokes, and isolated Chromium E2E passed. Before running Phase D against the migrated catalog, command-path inspection found `INC-016`: nominally diagnostic plan commands still opened RW and bootstrapped membership. Acceptance stopped without invoking them or changing the catalog; the enforced read-only correction must merge and be installed before Phase D resumes.
- phase_d_acceptance_2026_07_16: PR #19 merged after CI and Greptile 5/5, and the non-editable installed wheel was promoted from reviewed public `main`. Real-catalog Phase D passed with explicit paths and no `--apply`: 444 selected repositories remained visible; 390 valid manifests produced 494 requirements, 102 satisfied requirements, and 392 exact tasks across seven ledgers; 54 typed manifest-policy blockers and 95 copy-policy-drift diagnostics remained visible; no task was unassigned, no byte-capacity failure existed, and no satisfied requirement was scheduled again. All seven durable plan members remained present while currently offline. Byte hashes, sizes, mtimes, logical counts, schema version, integrity, foreign keys, capacity mode, library map, config, selection, and archive state were unchanged. Automation stopped before Phase E; no portal, service, or fill worker was started.
- phase_e_f_acceptance_2026_07_16: The loopback portal passed hostile-web, six-view, durable-fleet, idle-worker, and canonical-terminology checks. The operator selected `ark`, reviewed the cart, preserved all 54 artifact exclusions in DEF-030's public backlog, and finalized 390 supported repositories. After PR #21 merged, the hidden non-editable install was refreshed and final Phase-F CLI/Chromium checks passed: Library search and drive facets operate over 140 archived models, Fill progress is left-aligned, the reconciled plan is feasible with 383 ready and seven done, and all exact graph/ledger differences are explained. Fill was never started. Acceptance now stops before the separately attended verified restore.
- phase_g_preflight_2026_07_16: A read-only real-restore candidate audit found seven physically complete repositories whose ordinary Git-tracked metadata had no canonical restore hash. Restore correctly refused to equate presence with integrity. No restore, annex retrieval, catalog/archive mutation, or Fill ran. INC-017 owns universal ingestion hashing plus backup-first Git-object-validated legacy repair; Phase G remains stopped until that implementation is reviewed, installed, and separately approved against the migrated catalog.
- phase_g_remediation_review_2026_07_16: INC-017's universal ingestion hashing and backup-first, Git-object-validated `repair-hashes` workflow merged in PR #23 after green CI and Greptile 5/5. Phase G may resume only after that reviewed main revision is installed into the isolated acceptance runtime. The first real-catalog audit remains read-only; any apply and the disposable restore remain distinct operator approvals.
- phase_g_acceptance_2026_07_17: The installed reviewed build repaired 194/194 Git-proven legacy hash gaps after a consistent backup and separate operator approval. A repeated audit passed. The disposable restore reconstructed 12 files and 91,571,868 original bytes, preserved nested paths, and matched every original-byte hash before operator-approved cleanup. Phase G passed.
- phase_h_first_fill_2026_07_17: The non-resuming installed service and portal passed their gates and the operator explicitly started Fill. An invalid cached Hub OAuth credential first exposed INC-018; after operator re-login and a second explicit start, the first repository completed 11 missing files with six StreamZNN canaries (28.94 GB original, 19.21 GB stored). Dangling annex placeholders then exposed INC-019 and false network-stall cooldowns. The operator stopped cleanly; successful bytes were durable and synchronized, no child remained, and issue #28 plus DEC-046 gate the next reviewed install/resume.

### DEF-029: Defer collision-safe drive re-registration and dependency-aware retirement
- date: 2026-07-16 / status: active / triggered_by: RFC-001 Phase-E live drive-identity audit + operator request / related: DEC-014, DEC-017, DEC-019, DEC-030, DEC-036, DEC-042, INC-009, RFC-001 / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Replace label-keyed blind upsert with an identity-aware re-registration lifecycle. Registration must preflight an existing `drive_label` before changing a filesystem, annex remote, or catalog row. If filesystem UUID and annex UUID identify the same archive (with serial as supporting hardware evidence), refresh mutable metadata and the current mount-derived remote URL without deleting ownership records. If identity differs, fail closed and show the operator the old/new identity distinction plus every dependency: plan membership, `archived`, `replicas`, annex location/unique-key evidence, and configured remote. Offer a new label by default; permit explicit retirement/reuse only after filesystem work is complete and reconciliation proves that the old identity owns no required or unique bytes. Retirement must remove plan membership and stale annex-remote state before deleting the drive row in a controlled, backup-ready operation. Never infer staleness from offline, read-only, SMART-watch, or dock behavior, and never automatically overwrite or delete an existing identity.
- current_evidence: The migrated seven-drive catalog has no duplicate filesystem UUID, annex UUID, or serial. All seven labels remain intentional `ark` members. `drive-00` owns 2,311 durable `archived` rows and is not removable; the other labels currently own no `archived` or `replicas` rows but remain plan capacity and therefore are not orphans. Live `drive-00`, `drive-01`, and `drive-04` filesystem UUIDs match the migrated records. Drive 4 remains in-plan; its present read-only/SMART-watch state is treated as a dock/availability condition, not stale identity. The annex map URL for live Drives 1 and 4 still names the older `/mnt/drive-XX` mount while the desktop mounted them elsewhere, demonstrating that same-identity remote-path refresh is separate from record retirement.
- rationale: `register_drive()` currently updates the map remote and upserts `drives` by label while `archived`, `replicas`, and `plan_drives` retain that label. Reusing a label for different media can silently transfer durable byte claims and placement policy to the wrong filesystem; deleting merely-offline media can instead erase valid capacity/ownership. Identity comparison plus an explicit dependency report prevents both failures, while allowing harmless same-drive re-registration to repair stale mount URLs.
- interim: Do not reuse an existing label for different media. Re-register only when the mounted filesystem is known to be the same identity, and treat remote URL repair as metadata refresh. Keep shelved and temporarily degraded members in their plan unless a separate verified retirement is approved.
- revisit_when: before the next drive replacement, label reuse, or automated re-registration flow; implementation is required before ModelArk offers a remove/retire command or permits an existing label to bind to a different filesystem/annex identity.

### DEF-030: Return to excluded artifact repositories with full archive support
- date: 2026-07-16 / status: active / triggered_by: RFC-001 Phase-F cart review + operator confirmation that wanted repositories exist among the 54 manifest-policy exclusions / related: DEC-036, DEC-037, DEC-040, DEC-045, DEF-011, DEF-027, RFC-001 / standalone: `docs/deferred-artifact-support.md` / docs_updated: docs/deferred-artifact-support.md, docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Preserve the 54-repository acceptance snapshot as a public product backlog and revisit operator-chosen entries with full support. Do not weaken the safe public pickle default or add one-off filename exceptions merely to admit them. First refresh discovery and prefer an official supported safe release; otherwise implement the artifact family's canonical manifest, storage/capacity accounting, non-executing verification, atomic hash-checked restore, and mixed-cart/installed-wheel coverage. Pickle entries additionally require DEF-011 scanner, HF-scan, quarantine, and trusted-conversion work. The current acceptance cart remains at the safe 390-repository set while this work is deferred.
- rationale: The exclusions are capability gaps, not necessarily unwanted models. Keeping the names, observed formats, and cumulative reasons in the public product surface prevents the safe incident workaround from silently becoming permanent catalog loss, while retaining DEC-040's fail-closed acquisition boundary until each format has an end-to-end recovery contract.
- revisit_when: the operator prioritizes any listed repository for the next fill, an upstream repository publishes safetensors/GGUF, or implementation begins on pickle, ONNX, tokenizer-only, MLX, or another observed artifact family.

### DEF-031: Explain rotated CSRF capability to stale portal tabs
- date: 2026-07-16 / status: active / triggered_by: RFC-001 Phase-F portal restart / related: DEC-038, RFC-001 / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, docs/rfcs/001-migrated-runtime-acceptance.md
- decision: Preserve DEC-038's per-process CSRF rotation and fail-closed mutation behavior. When a tab survives a portal restart, a rejected mutation must instead present an operator-facing “portal restarted; refresh and retry” state. The client must not automatically replay the rejected mutation after obtaining a new capability, because replay could duplicate or silently perform an operator action after context changed.
- current_evidence: A pre-restart tab attempted to select the active plan with the prior process's capability and received the raw missing/invalid-CSRF error. A hard refresh obtained the new non-cacheable index capability. A non-mutating same-origin JSON request with that fresh token passed Host, Origin, content-type, and CSRF checks and reached routing, proving the security boundary was healthy and no plan/catalog mutation occurred.
- revisit_when: the portal's shared POST helper or global error surface is next changed; add a stale-tab browser regression covering server-token rotation without mutation replay.

### INC-018: Invalid cached Hub credential churned a repository batch before failing
- date: 2026-07-17 / status: fixed by DEC-046 (PR #29; live validated 2026-07-17) / triggered_by: RFC-001 Phase-H first Fill / related: DEC-003, DEC-019, DEC-023, DEC-045, DEC-046, INC-004, RFC-001 / docs_updated: modelark/fetch.py, modelark/fill.py, modelark/cli.py, modelark/web/fill_api.py, modelark/web/static/fill.js, tests/test_replan.py, tests/test_watchdog.py, tests/test_e2e_portal.py, docs/fill_pipeline.md, docs/rfcs/001-migrated-runtime-acceptance.md, docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: A cached browser OAuth credential had expired or been revoked. Authenticated requests to public Hub files returned HTTP 401 while anonymous requests succeeded. Fill rapidly rotated across 13 repositories, repeated the batch, and reported the second failure of one repository as `FETCH_TASK_FAILED`, even though every repository shared the same credential failure.
- impact: No unsafe byte or catalog mutation occurred, but the executor wasted requests and presented the wrong recovery target. The operator could reasonably investigate the named model instead of refreshing or removing the systemic credential.
- remediation: Production CLI and portal contexts validate a configured credential once at Gate A before reconciliation or archive writes. No configured token remains valid for public repositories. Only a definitive HTTP 401 becomes `HF_AUTH_INVALID`; transient validation failures stay on the bounded network path. A 401 observed after preflight stops the current batch immediately and carries credential-source evidence plus explicit recovery actions. The UI labels retry counts as transient network retries rather than generic stalls.
- acceptance_boundary: Do not persist, print, or commit token material. Do not require authentication for public-only operation. A future credential refresh still requires an explicit operator retry; ModelArk does not replay a rejected mutation or automatically resume Fill.

### INC-019: Dangling annex placeholders were mistaken for transient download stalls
- date: 2026-07-17 / status: fixed by DEC-046 (PR #29; live validated 2026-07-17) / triggered_by: RFC-001 Phase-H first Fill after INC-018 recovery / related: DEC-003, DEC-006, DEC-019, DEC-023, DEC-036, DEC-037, DEC-045, DEC-046, INC-004, INC-010, RFC-001 / docs_updated: modelark/download_worker.py, modelark/fetch.py, modelark/fill.py, modelark/web/fill_api.py, modelark/web/static/fill.js, tests/test_annex_placeholder_recovery.py, tests/test_replan.py, tests/test_watchdog.py, tests/test_e2e_portal.py, docs/fill_pipeline.md, docs/rfcs/001-migrated-runtime-acceptance.md, docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: A repository worktree contained a dangling git-annex symlink whose object was absent, while annex location history still contained a stale claim. `hf_hub_download(local_dir=final_model_dir)` followed the broken symlink and raised local `FileNotFoundError`. The isolated worker classified the generic exception as transient, causing repeated watchdog cooldowns and then the same failure on the next repository.
- impact: Fill could not repair canonical files already represented by dangling annex placeholders and attributed a deterministic local namespace conflict to network instability. Completed prior work remained durable, but the operator received long delays and weak evidence.
- remediation: Downloads resume in a private deterministic staging directory on the target archive filesystem, never through the final worktree path. ModelArk verifies the original-byte hash and compression canary there, then atomically publishes only when the target is absent, already has identical verified bytes, or is a dangling symlink that `git annex lookupkey` proves belongs to that archive. Arbitrary broken links, mismatched files, directories, cross-filesystem staging, and local filesystem errors fail closed with typed evidence and no cooldown. Annex add and catalog recording occur only after publication.
- safety_boundary: The repair is per-file and proof-driven. It never bulk-deletes placeholders, never treats stale `whereis` history as byte presence, never replaces an unproven path, and never promotes a partial or unverified download. Completion continues to derive only from durable `archived` rows and physical verification.

### DEC-046: Verified same-filesystem staging is the fetch publication boundary
- date: 2026-07-17 / status: accepted and implemented by PR #29; live validated / triggered_by: INC-018 + INC-019 during RFC-001 Phase H / related: DEC-003, DEC-006, DEC-019, DEC-023, DEC-036, DEC-037, DEC-045, INC-004, INC-010, INC-018, INC-019, RFC-001 / docs_updated: modelark/download_worker.py, modelark/fetch.py, modelark/fill.py, modelark/cli.py, modelark/web/fill_api.py, modelark/web/static/fill.js, tests/test_annex_placeholder_recovery.py, tests/test_replan.py, tests/test_watchdog.py, tests/test_e2e_portal.py, docs/fill_pipeline.md, docs/rfcs/001-migrated-runtime-acceptance.md, docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Separate acquisition from publication. Each missing file downloads to deterministic private same-filesystem staging, is checked against authoritative/computed original-byte SHA-256, and completes any bounded compression plus round-trip canary before the verified stored bytes cross into the archive worktree. Publication uses atomic replacement. A dangling target is replaceable only when the current annex repository proves it is an annex-managed placeholder; an existing identical verified file is reused, and every other collision is a typed terminal conflict that preserves both sides.
- failure_model: Local path, permission, read-only, ENOSPC, and I/O errors are not network retries. A configured Hub credential rejected with HTTP 401 is systemic, not repository-specific. Both stop the batch immediately with typed evidence and actions. Only actual transient transport failures receive bounded retries/cooldowns, and the operator UI must name the retry reason and attempt count.
- durable_truth: Staging and retry state remain ephemeral and never imply completion. Annex ingestion and the `archived` row follow successful atomic publication; the DEC-045 reconciler continues to derive work from canonical manifests plus durable catalog/physical facts. This retains crash recovery without a persisted work queue.
- rejected: Downloading directly through final worktree paths; unlinking every broken symlink before acquisition; trusting annex location logs as proof that bytes exist; copying staged bytes across filesystems before validation; treating all worker exceptions as transient; and automatically resuming after credential repair.

### INC-020: Repository-specific gated access surfaced as a generic fetch-task failure
- date: 2026-07-17 / status: remediated via DEC-047 (PR #31; live validated 2026-07-22) / triggered_by: live RFC-001 Fill after DEC-046 validation / related: DEF-010, DEF-021, DEF-023, DEC-033, DEC-045, DEC-047, INC-018, RFC-001 / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, modelark/fetch.py, modelark/fill.py, modelark/verifier.py, modelark/web/fill_worker.py, modelark/web/fill_api.py, modelark/web/static/index.html, modelark/web/static/fill.js, modelark/web/static/verify.js, tests/test_replan.py, tests/test_verifier.py, tests/test_oopsie.py, tests/test_e2e_portal.py
- symptom: `ai21labs/AI21-Jamba-Mini-1.7` returned Hugging Face `GatedRepoError` at 13:34 and again at 19:27. The first response existed only in logs; both consumed the generic per-repository failure budget. Fill safely completed 711.6 GB of source work / 529.3 GB stored, then stopped at 21:00 as `FETCH_TASK_FAILED`. The expected typed terminal was subsequently overwritten in `last_fill.json` as `UNHANDLED_FILL_ERROR`, yielding irrelevant inspect-logs/report-bug actions.
- impact: No archive bytes were corrupt or suspect, and all completed files remained durable, but the operator could neither see the first access block nor act at the second. Retrying unchanged access was pointless, the terminal named the wrong failure class, and Verify had no durable access follow-up.
- fix: Implement DEC-047. Preserve expected typed executor terminals rather than routing them through the crash guard. This incident is distinct from INC-018: a valid account token can download public repositories while one repository still requires license/access acceptance.

### DEC-047: Gated repository access is an operator decision and typed follow-up, not a fetch retry
- date: 2026-07-17 / status: accepted and implemented by PR #31; live validated 2026-07-22 / triggered_by: INC-020 + operator direction / related: DEF-010, DEF-021, DEF-023, DEC-033, DEC-045, INC-018, INC-020 / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, modelark/fetch.py, modelark/fill.py, modelark/verifier.py, modelark/web/fill_worker.py, modelark/web/fill_api.py, modelark/web/server.py, modelark/web/static/index.html, modelark/web/static/app.css, modelark/web/static/fill.js, modelark/web/static/verify.js, tests/test_replan.py, tests/test_verifier.py, tests/test_oopsie.py, tests/test_e2e_portal.py
- decision: Within one Fill session, the first repository-specific gated response emits a retained portal notice/toast and lets other feasible work continue. A second response publishes a single operator prompt containing a fixed-origin Hugging Face repository link and waits up to five minutes for exactly one matching `retry` or `skip` action. Retry reconciles and reattempts without consuming the generic network/task-failure budget. Skip or timeout parks the repository for the remainder of that session, records an append-only typed access event, and continues other work. When only parked gated work remains, Fill completes as `PLAN_COMPLETE_WITH_FOLLOWUPS`, never as a false satisfied plan or generic error.
- followup_model: The existing Verify suspect projection becomes a typed operator follow-up queue. `integrity` entries continue to mean archived bytes warrant re-verification; `access-gated` explicitly means no bytes landed and repository access is pending. Access-only rows offer the Hub link and are excluded from Re-verify All. A later successful `archived` event resolves the access follow-up automatically. Skip/timeout never changes selection, so a later Start naturally retries unresolved durable work.
- safety_boundary: The work graph and encounter counters remain ephemeral per DEC-045. Only append-only fetch evidence is durable; no persisted task or target is invented. Prompt responses are bound to an active opaque id so stale tabs cannot answer a later decision. A five-minute timeout continues safely rather than holding a multi-day fill indefinitely. Global credential rejection remains the immediate typed Gate-A boundary from INC-018.

### INC-021: Non-recursive `.incomplete` glob false-killed nested-path downloads and leaked their partials
- date: 2026-07-19 / status: remediated under DEC-023 (PR #33; deployed in LocalModelArk b64f37b) / triggered_by: RFC-001 Phase-H Fill after service resume / related: INC-004, INC-010, DEC-023, DEF-026, RFC-001 / docs_updated: modelark/fetch.py, tests/test_watchdog.py, docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: Resuming Fill, `black-forest-labs/FLUX.2-dev` shard `transformer/diffusion_pytorch_model-00001-of-00007.safetensors` (9.94 GB, LFS/xet) emitted four `transient download retry` events at near-exact 180 s intervals with `reason="transient_network"`, though the network was healthy. The download child was actively writing eight parallel `.incomplete` segments (one already 2.7 GB) under `download/transformer/`, yet the no-progress watchdog read zero and killed the child at each `_DL_STALL_SECS` boundary.
- root_cause: `_download_shard`'s `progress()` and `_sweep_incomplete` both globbed `dl_cache.glob("*.incomplete")` — non-recursive. `huggingface_hub` writes a nested `rfilename`'s partial to `download/<subdir>/<hash>.incomplete`, which a top-level glob never matches. Any repo file with a path separator whose transfer exceeds 180 s was therefore false-killed and, on hf_xet, restarted from zero (INC-010). Top-level `rfilename`s (e.g. `ai21labs/Jamba-v0.1` shards) and nested files that finish under 180 s were unaffected, which hid the defect until a large nested shard resumed.
- impact: No archive-integrity risk — the write-time hash/canary gate is downstream, so nothing corrupt is stored. But the affected repo is repeatedly retried and then isolated despite a good connection, bandwidth is re-spent each attempt, the operator sees a wrong `transient_network` diagnosis, and the sibling `_sweep_incomplete` could not reclaim nested orphans: this single incident leaked ~65 GB of `download/transformer/*.incomplete` partials on the archive drive.
- fix: Both probes recurse (`rglob("*.incomplete")`), so download liveness and orphan reclaim both see nested per-`rfilename` subdirectories. Regression tests in `tests/test_watchdog.py` assert the shard's `progress()` counts a nested `.incomplete` and that `_sweep_incomplete` reclaims nested plus top-level partials.
- safety_boundary: The change only widens where the watchdog and sweep look; it does not alter the stall threshold, the retry/cooldown budget, or the write-time integrity gates. Genuine hangs are still killed at `_DL_STALL_SECS`.

### DEC-048: Ship catalog seeding as the first onboarding increment
- date: 2026-07-20 / status: accepted and implemented (PR pending); tagged 0.2.0 / triggered_by: operator identified that a fresh install cannot start without re-walking the whole Hub, a hard first-run blocker as external attention grows / related: DEC-002, DEC-045, DEF-003, DEF-025 / docs_updated: modelark/seed.py, modelark/cli.py, modelark/data/catalog_seed.jsonl, pyproject.toml, tests/test_seed.py, README.md, CHANGELOG.md, docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Add `modelark import`, which seeds the `models` table from a sanitized JSONL export — the committed `catalog/export/models.jsonl`, now also packaged into the wheel as `modelark/data/catalog_seed.jsonl` so installed (non-checkout) users seed offline with no Hugging Face token. Source resolution is explicit `--from` → source checkout → packaged resource. Import is insert-only by default (`--refresh` overwrites) so re-running never downgrades a row a richer local `discover` produced. Non-scalar export values (notably `tags`, a JSON array) are stored as JSON text, matching `discover.persist_info`.
- scope_boundary: The sanitized export ships model rows only; per-file rows (`files`) are deliberately not distributed, so seeding supports browse and model-level (`total_size_bytes`) budgeting immediately, while per-file manifests are fetched on demand for the repositories actually selected — consistent with "catalog metadata broadly, fetch details for what you curate." A `test_packaged_seed_matches_committed_export` guard fails on drift between the two copies when run from a checkout.
- onboarding_direction: This is the first slice of a larger onboarding surface. The agreed shape (deferred to the 0.3 line): one read-only-first onboarding engine exposing an ordered, self-detecting, re-entrant list of typed setup steps, rendered by both a CLI (`doctor`/`setup`) and a portal Setup view over the same shared core — the established thin-wrapper pattern. Each step declares an automatability class (auto / auto-confirm / manual-command / manual-interactive) so the surface never silently performs a root or destructive action: apt installs and the `smartctl` sudoers drop-in stay hand-off commands (DEF-025), and browser-side drive formatting is deferred in favour of emitting the guarded CLI command (labelled alpha). Lab-collection and category-bundle presets for guided catalog building will live in a new packaged `collections.yaml`, promoting the wishlist's existing org clusters and architecture-derived categories (DEC-002) into intent-level selections. Extends DEF-003's deferred onboarding qualification.
- revisit_when: building the 0.3 onboarding engine (`doctor`/`setup` + portal Setup view), or when the shipped seed is regenerated (`modelark export` then copy into `modelark/data/`).

### RFC-002: First-class placement approval and execution control
- date: 2026-07-20 / status: accepted; implementation not started, binding DEC and issue rewrites next / triggered_by: mid-fill GLM-5.2 addition exposed unsafe free-space evidence, partial pinning, silent batch-boundary re-placement, missing drive lifecycle operations, and no revision-bound selection preview / related: DEC-019, DEC-030, DEC-034, DEC-045, DEC-046, DEC-047, DEF-022, DEF-028, DEF-029, INC-014, INC-018, INC-019, INC-020, INC-021, RFC-001, issues #35–#39 / standalone: `docs/rfcs/002-first-class-placement-approval.md` / docs_updated: docs/rfcs/002-first-class-placement-approval.md, docs/plans/placement-capacity-hardening.md, docs/decision_log.md
- proposal: Introduce one immutable in-memory `PlannerInput`, pure requirement/candidate/feasibility/placement functions, normalized immutable placement proposals, a global `planner_revision` fast approval CAS backed by authoritative semantic recomputation, deterministic monotonic-only execution projection, and a minimal durable execution session with fencing token plus same-host per-drive `flock`. Durable `archived` facts remain completion truth; the executor cannot invoke placement or silently remap approved targets.
- scope_boundary: Add only five planning/control tables (`planner_state`, `placement_proposals`, `proposal_tasks`, `proposal_files`, `execution_sessions`) in addition to issue-owned #35/#37 storage facts. Normalized rows are authoritative; canonical proposal JSON is computed and only its versioned hash is stored. Snapshot existing manifest structure, LFS SHA-256, and task-relevant/reused hash evidence rather than pinning provider commits. Cross-host writers, distributed leases, provider artifact snapshots, event sourcing, multi-plan concurrency, and a transport rewrite are out of scope.
- runtime_behavior: Current batch-boundary self-replacement becomes an explicit `APPROVED_PLACEMENT_NO_LONGER_FEASIBLE` stop-and-repreview terminal. Monotonic progress, guided hot-swap await, evidence refresh that preserves fit, DEC-047 gated session parking, stop, and throttle retain approval without re-solving. Changed identity/epoch/manifest/policy, expanded work, or known approved-target shortfall requires fresh operator approval.
- migration_boundary: Treat cutover as RFC-001-grade: prefer the live GLM/BF16 Fill to drain, stop every writer at a Fill-idle boundary, preserve raw/consistent/SQL backups and hashes, rehearse on a copy, publish transactionally, and start without auto-resume. Migration preserves selection and archived truth but creates no approval or clean-anchor evidence; the current finalized selection requires one explicit preview/approval before Fill resumes. Old binaries must refuse the newer schema.
- integrity_residual: Full-manifest hashes catch catalog manifest drift; explicit tasks cannot broaden to unlisted upstream additions; removed/renamed paths fail lookup; every downloaded original must match its approved byte length before publish; LFS weights retain upstream hash enforcement; and every ingested original retains its restore hash. A same-path, same-size content change to an upstream-hashless Git-tracked file may still follow provider HEAD; provider commit pinning is deferred to a separate RFC/issue.
- acceptance: Preserve DEC-046/047 and INC-018–021 behavior through pre-refactor characterization/fault tests and RFC-001-style attended installed-runtime acceptance. Copied-catalog replay proves planning/proposal/projection and migration equivalence but is not transport/crash evidence. Stop on any expanded/remapped projection, optimizer call from commit/start, unapproved migrated Fill, silent evidence-driven target change, dual proposal source of truth, or lost transport safety terminal.
- review_update_2026_07_20: The conditional architecture review is resolved and RFC-002 is accepted. Live `starting`/`running`/`stopping` sessions exclusively block planner graph mutations; `paused`/`blocked` are non-live, release lease and physical locks, and permit the reconciliation needed for recovery before authoritative resume validation. Active-plan switching supersedes/clears approval and requires a fresh approval. Recomputed requirements and semantic hashes are authoritative while `planner_revision` is only a fast CAS pre-check. Interim portal-guard limits, compression-ratio overrun routing, and post-safety-floor structural-capacity precedence are explicit acceptance requirements.
- review_update_2026_07_20_greptile: A paused/blocked/terminal execution-session row is immutable and is never reactivated. Resume creates one new `starting` row with a new fencing token, the same still-valid approved proposal, and a `resumed_from_session_id` audit link to its predecessor; the no-live-session CAS prevents concurrent successors. This resolves PR #40's session-lifecycle ambiguity without adding another table or changing proposal lifetime.
- pseudocode_update_2026_07_20: RFC-002 now carries implementation-grade pseudocode for fact/evidence capture, pure requirements/candidates, the mixed-evidence Gate-B ladder, deterministic `tiered_v2`, draft publication, approval CAS, monotonic projection, new-row resume lineage, fixed-map execution, revision/token writes, drive mutation, recovery, and canonical serialization. The pseudocode adds no platform scope: it pins the reviewed authority and transaction seams. Immutable proposal hashing excludes lifecycle metadata; semantic hashes exclude volatile capacity observations, which are revalidated against the exact assignment; per-file live-free evidence shares one sorted physical-fence scope with dirtying through clean-anchor publication; and a clean anchor stores raw filesystem-available bytes so the versioned safety floor is applied exactly once when deriving admission free.
- pseudocode_review_update_2026_07_20: The implementation contract now reserves paired nullable dirty-generation owner session/token fields in #35 for #39 recovery; makes full projection start/resume plus drive-batch/event based rather than per-file/task; adds first-class `adopt_current` approval for unchanged migrated selections; publishes the complete Gate-B preview outcome/action table; requires conservative revision bumps unless a canonical no-op is proven; and pins DEC-047 same-task retry priority plus failure-budget exemption. Pseudocode naming is deliberately concise and contextual—module/type contracts carry detail instead of sentence-length function names.
- pseudocode_second_review_update_2026_07_21: The implementation contract now serializes dirty-generation start and expired-session recovery under the catalog-scoped controller fence, records an actual worker claim separately from the hosting controller, and requires monitored children to inherit drive-fence FDs so parent death cannot permit overlapping recovery. Every desired requirement receives a proposal row, including exact `baseline_satisfied` certificates that make loss of pre-approved completion a projection violation. Start freezes one hash-validated execution config; transport never rereads global config. Compression-aware execution charges cumulative actual durable bytes plus a recomputed remaining forecast without full per-file reprojection, and resume reconstructs that spend from durable proposal-relative progress. DEC-047's first gated notice, prompt retry/skip/timeout, and prompt-stop behavior remain explicit. Normal successful mutation close reconciles only the dirty generation's touched write set unless registration/recovery/drift/repair explicitly requests full inventory, live-session uniqueness is global, and the mixed-evidence infeasibility result is named narrowly so it does not overclaim that freeing known capacity cannot help.

### DEC-049: Previewed placement is approved atomically and executed without re-solving
- date: 2026-07-21 / status: accepted; binding for RFC-002 and issues #35–#39 / triggered_by: the GLM-5.2 mid-fill placement incident and RFC-002 architecture review / related: DEC-019, DEC-030, DEC-034, DEC-045, DEC-046, DEC-047, DEF-022, DEF-028, DEF-029, INC-014, INC-018, INC-019, INC-020, INC-021, RFC-002, issues #35–#39 / docs_updated: docs/rfcs/002-first-class-placement-approval.md, docs/plans/placement-capacity-hardening.md, docs/decision_log.md
- decision: ModelArk captures catalog facts, policy/config, identity, and capacity evidence once in an immutable `PlannerInput`; pure requirement, candidate, feasibility, and versioned placement functions produce one exact normalized proposal. Approval freshly recomputes semantic facts and validates that exact assignment, then atomically applies the reviewed selection mutation and active-approval transition under a revision CAS. Start/resume derives only a deterministic monotonic subset of the approved work. Commit, projection, and execution never invoke placement or substitute another target/source.
- capacity_authority: Admission uses a single evidence model. An identity-proven mounted volume is authoritative only while the required writer fence is held; a clean offline anchor is authoritative only for the same identity/capacity epoch under continuous exclusive control; dirty, unfenced-shared, stale, or unproven evidence is unknown. Clean anchors are append-only observations of raw filesystem-available bytes, the safety floor is applied exactly once, and `drives.free_bytes` becomes compatibility/diagnostic data rather than admission authority. Unknown drives contribute zero executable capacity and may receive only post-safety-floor usable maxima in non-executable sensitivity analysis.
- durable_truth: `files`, `archived`, verified physical/annex facts, drive identity epochs, dirty generations, and clean anchors remain storage truth. Proposals authorize exact work but never satisfy it. Every desired requirement receives one proposal row, including an exact certificate for work already satisfied at approval. Progress may remove work only; loss, expansion, remapping, manifest drift, identity drift, or a budget envelope overrun fails closed with a typed result. No archived row is auto-deleted to manufacture feasibility, offline is not lost, and retired identity is never implicitly reinstated.
- execution_control: At most one execution session is live globally. The controller identity and actual worker claim are distinct; every catalog mutation validates the monotonically increasing fencing token. Physical mutation order is catalog-scoped controller flock → sorted identity-derived drive flocks → short SQLite transaction. Dirty state is published before allocation, monitored children inherit the held drive-fence descriptors, normal close reconciles only the generation's touched write set, and recovery cannot terminalize an expired session until the actual worker and every possible child writer are excluded on every proposal-mutatable drive. Terminal rows are immutable; resume creates a new session and token.
- operator_contract: A live session blocks planner graph mutation. Evidence-compatible progress, guided hot-swap await, DEC-047 gated deferral/parking, stop, and throttle preserve approval without re-solving. A changed requirement/manifest/config/identity/epoch or an exact approved-target shortfall stops at a safe boundary and requires a fresh preview. Migration fabricates neither approval nor clean evidence; the existing selection needs one explicit `adopt_current` preview/approval at a Fill-idle cutover.
- scope_and_delivery: This is a functional-core/imperative-shell refactor around the protected DEC-046/047 transport, not a transport rewrite, provider-versioning system, distributed lease, event store, or multi-plan executor. Deliver the portal mutation guard independently; then #35 → logical #36a → #38 → #37 schema/gating → #39 → separate #37 operations on the isolated fix branch. Preserve original GitHub issue bodies: append authoritative scope/acceptance comments and later PR links. Every phase is tests-first, with incident characterization and failing contract/migration/fault tests reviewable before production changes. #39 additionally requires a copied-catalog projection benchmark with a fixed numerical p95 and bounded call count before its implementation PR is accepted.
- issue_contracts: Published append-only binding comments on [#35](https://github.com/Auspex-Aerie/modelark/issues/35#issuecomment-5037967073), [#36](https://github.com/Auspex-Aerie/modelark/issues/36#issuecomment-5037967195), [#37](https://github.com/Auspex-Aerie/modelark/issues/37#issuecomment-5037967307), [#38](https://github.com/Auspex-Aerie/modelark/issues/38#issuecomment-5037967462), and [#39](https://github.com/Auspex-Aerie/modelark/issues/39#issuecomment-5037967599). #35 pins catalog-v3 clean-anchor/dirty-generation DDL and transitions. #39 pins the copied-fixture performance gate at p95 ≤2.0 seconds for full capture/recompute/projection and ≤500 ms for pure projection after 5 warm-ups over 30 measured runs. Implementation PR links are appended later; original issue bodies remain unchanged.
- workflow_update_2026_07_21: The operator supersedes the earlier direct-to-main guard and periodic-main-sync workflow. The portal guard remains a small independent first PR, but it and every later phase PR target the long-lived `fix/placement-capacity-hardening` integration branch. Do not merge/rebase/cherry-pick `main` into that branch during the review series without explicit operator approval. Each PR uses tests-first commits, Greptile iteration, and mandatory operator/reviewer stop gates; only the final copied-catalog/fault/rollback-evidenced integration PR targets `main`. The executable handoff is `docs/plans/placement-capacity-implementor-handoff.md`.
- workflow_issue_record: The append-only [#39 workflow correction](https://github.com/Auspex-Aerie/modelark/issues/39#issuecomment-5038287607) supersedes only the earlier contract comment's direct-to-main PR target; issue body and prior comments remain unchanged.

### INC-022: A full-drive transition displayed Drive-02 as downloading instead of clearly awaiting it
- date: 2026-07-22 / status: contained — Fill stopped; failed drive not yet excluded or re-planned / triggered_by: attended LocalModelArk Fill on 2026-07-21–22 + operator-observed Drive-02 failure on connection / related: INC-008, DEC-026, DEC-047, DEC-049, RFC-002, issues #35, #37, #39 / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: `drive-01` filled and could not admit its final two planned models. During the resulting transition the portal showed `Drive-02` trying to download rather than clearly paused at an insert/await boundary, and subsequent work appeared to be skipped. When the operator connected `Drive-02` on the morning of July 22, the drive failed and is no longer available; other fleet drives remain candidates for a replacement plan.
- evidence: The running checkout was `LocalModelArk` commit `b64f37b`. The operator's contemporaneous display observation is the only historical target evidence: the legacy executor persists neither assignments nor transition events. Its retained application log contains uninterrupted July 21–22 file work and the operator stop at `2026-07-22 11:53:31`, but logs neither `plan-capacity` transitions nor a `Drive-02` selection/attempt and contains no `awaiting drive` event. The missing telemetry prevents a log-only determination of whether the executor selected `Drive-02`, the queue projected it, or the display retained incompatible fields during re-plan/await. The display defect is concrete: `FillWorker._emit` merge-updates one long-lived state dictionary, capacity/await events do not clear stale `file_phase`, `file`, `repo`, and `drive` fields, and `renderTelemetry` labels that mixed state "Now fetching". Thus a prior download can remain visibly active during a later capacity/re-plan/await state. The catalog records 896 files verified on `drive-01` during the interval and no archived file on `drive-02` ever. Its `drive-01.free_bytes` still equals the registration-time full capacity despite about 7.388 TB of stored archive rows, while `drive-02` still says `health='ok'` from `last_seen='2026-07-11 16:25:30'`; both are stale diagnostics, not historical execution evidence. On its final recovery attempt the operator could no longer read even the drive's filesystem links and estimated it survived only about three additional power cycles before failing permanently. That operator-observed terminal media/device condition is sufficient to classify this identity as lost even though ModelArk captured no I/O event. Separately, `fetch_events` records two gated responses for `ai21labs/AI21-Jamba-Mini-1.5` followed by the configured five-minute timeout and session-local park, so some apparent skipping was legitimate DEC-047 behavior but was not distinguished clearly from the drive transition.
- analysis_correction: A post-stop read-only reconstruction placed seven tasks on `drive-01` before 204 tasks (about 3.516 TB guaranteed budget) on `drive-02`. That snapshot is useful only as a current sensitivity result. It cannot establish the overnight order because the legacy executor re-solves at batch/capacity boundaries and, once the drives were absent, fell back to stale catalog capacity. Do not use that reconstruction to contradict the live display observation or to conclude that `Drive-02` was never reached.
- impact: No ModelArk archive bytes were lost with `drive-02` because it held zero durable `archived` facts, and completed `drive-01` files remain durable. The operator could not tell whether Fill was downloading, re-planning, awaiting a target, or session-parking gated work, and the retained evidence cannot reconstruct that decision afterward. The failed target's approximately 3.516 TB current provisional assignment cannot proceed and requires fresh placement rather than a legacy silent re-home.
- containment_and_evidence: The operator stopped Fill cleanly. The complete runtime log was copied byte-for-byte to the intentionally ignored local file `logs/INC-022-localmodelark-2026-07-21-22-modelark.log` (1,436,545 bytes; SHA-256 `5755dec0ac6e1d943d31c729763e537d177c4cc1cfb30a4ef2c8b94b48654630`). Do not resume against the failed target or manually delete its identity/catalog provenance. Establish current identity-proven capacity evidence for the remaining drives, explicitly mark/exclude the failed identity when the lifecycle operation exists, and require a fresh preview/approval before replacement work starts.
- acceptance_regression: Preserve separate tests and operator-visible evidence for (1) a full-drive preflight transitioning to an exact next target, (2) an approved target that is merely offline and must be awaited without remapping, (3) a target explicitly declared lost and removed from satisfaction/candidacy before fresh placement, (4) gated session parking that names the repository and cannot be confused with drive omission, and (5) mutually exclusive status snapshots plus logs that retain the selected target, batch/task context, typed reason, and next operator action. A transition must explicitly clear fields from the prior activity so an old `download` cannot render during `plan-capacity` or `awaiting-drive`. This incident does not expand PR-03a's dormant mutation-envelope slice; it supplies acceptance evidence for #35 capacity truth, #37 lifecycle operations, and #39 fixed-map execution.

### DEC-050: Await an offline target; explicitly exclude and re-approve a lost target
- date: 2026-07-22 / status: accepted; implementation bound to RFC-002 issues #35, #37, and #39 / triggered_by: INC-022 + DEC-049 / related: INC-007, INC-008, DEC-026, DEC-047, DEF-022, RFC-002 / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Treat temporary absence and confirmed loss as different operator states. An approved target whose identity is unchanged and whose evidence remains admissible is `offline`: guided execution retains the exact approved tasks and waits at GATE A until that target returns; it never omits, substitutes, or re-solves them. A target the operator confirms has failed is `lost`: stop the current execution, preserve its identity and provenance, explicitly mark-lost/exclude it from candidates and copy satisfaction, derive remaining work from durable facts, and require a fresh preview/approval over identity-proven evidence from the remaining fleet. No timeout, stale `last_seen`, missing mount, health string, or failed probe may automatically promote `offline` to `lost`.
- observability_contract: Planning and execution surfaces must distinguish `active transfer`, `capacity transition`, `queued on another approved batch`, `awaiting approved target`, `session-parked gated work`, and `lost target requires fresh placement`. Each transition publishes a coherent activity snapshot or explicitly clears incompatible prior fields; merge-only UI state must never render a stale download as current. Retain the target identity/label, batch or task context, typed reason, and operator action in both logs and status so an operator can tell re-planning, waiting, and omission apart without reconstructing the catalog by hand.
- incident_application: `Drive-02` has no archived facts, so explicit loss will not invalidate a unique stored copy. It does invalidate the old provisional placement of its missing work. A replacement plan may use other drives only after current authoritative evidence proves feasibility; legacy `free_bytes` and the failed drive's stale `health='ok'` row are not sufficient. Until the #37 lifecycle operation and #39 approval path ship, keep the live Fill stopped rather than editing plan/catalog rows ad hoc or relying on the legacy executor to silently re-home work.
- scope_boundary: This decision reiterates RFC-002's offline≠lost and no-silent-remap invariants in response to live evidence. It adds no distributed lease, automatic hardware diagnosis, transport rewrite, or PR-03a implementation scope.
- 2026-07-31 UPDATE — **Drive-02 is operator-declared lost; additional hardware is test evidence, not loss authority.** The operator's explicit declaration is sufficient to drive the manual mark-lost/exclude workflow when that operation is exercised; reconnecting or power-cycling the known dying device is not required to legitimise the state change. Detection may gather evidence and recommend an action, but no missing mount, failed probe, SMART result, I/O error, timeout, or absent device may automatically promote `offline` to `lost`. Testing should be stronger than one historical device: begin with deterministic fault injection or disposable images, then use the operator's other dying/dead drives in attended, read-only physical trials covering absent/unrecognised hardware, enumeration without mount, readable identity with failing health evidence, intermittent I/O, and a wrong identity appearing under a known label. Every trial binds observations and any manual action to the exact persisted identity/epoch rather than the display label, performs no archive writes, and preserves Drive-02 from unnecessary additional stress.

### DEC-051: Fill content satisfaction requires a recorded digest; presence alone never proves durability
- date: 2026-07-26 / status: superseded by DEC-055; implemented as written in PR-09 (#39-B) Gate 2 and accepted at that tip / triggered_by: PR-09 Gate-2 human review finding F37-a — `fill._archive_content_satisfies` treated an unhashed approved file as durably satisfied on archive-row presence alone / related: INC-017, DIS-002, DEC-003, DEC-019, DEC-049, RFC-002, modelark/fill.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, modelark/fill.py
- decision: One content-satisfaction rule governs both replica source readiness and target durable satisfaction. (1) When an approved `proposal_files.orig_sha256` is present, the `archived` row must carry the same non-null `orig_sha256`; inequality or a null archive hash is not satisfaction. (2) When the approved hash is absent — ordinary for non-LFS git blobs, for which Hugging Face publishes no content digest (`modelark/core/schema.sql:46`) — satisfaction requires the `archived` row to carry its own ingestion-computed `orig_sha256`. (3) When both the approved and the archived hash are null, the file is neither satisfied nor source-ready; it fails closed. Presence of an `archived` row never by itself constitutes durable content satisfaction, on either side of a copy.
- rationale: INC-017 already established for restore that presence is not integrity, and its remediation made ingestion always persist a computed original-byte digest even when the Hub supplies none; DIS-002 records that stored sha256 as the standing guarantee. Fill must honour the same guarantee restore depends on, or it will skip work that restore later refuses to verify. Requiring an approved-side hash instead is not implementable: no digest exists pre-download for non-LFS files, and because the null sits on the approved side, re-fetching never clears it, so that rule would never terminate. A size or `is_lfs` threshold was rejected because `proposal_files` carries no `is_lfs` column — gating on it would need either a new schema version or a read of the mutable `files` table, which RFC-002 file authority forbids.
- impact: `fill._archive_content_satisfies` and both callers (`_source_files_content_ready`, and target evaluation in `_projection_work_units`) change behaviour only for the null-approved/null-archived case. Measured per target drive on the operator-approved 390-repository acceptance fixture, across the approved proposal's 9,520 proposal-file rows: 4,332 unhashed rows have no archived row on their target and are already counted missing; 282 unhashed rows satisfy under this rule because the archive carries an ingestion-computed digest; and 265 unhashed rows newly fail closed because neither side holds a digest — all 265 on `drive-00`, and none on `drive-01`, `drive-02`, `drive-03`, or `drive-06`. On the replica source side, joined on `source_drive`, 497 of 1,887 replica-task file rows are unhashed against a null source digest and will hold at `waiting_dependency` instead of copying. An earlier reviewer estimate of roughly 1,541 affected target rows came from a drive-label-agnostic join and overcounted by about six times; the figures here supersede it. The affected rows are INC-017's defect class; `repair-hashes` is the presumed remediation path and its actual coverage must be confirmed rather than assumed. Regressions are required for all four hash combinations on both the source and target sides.
- citation_correction: the shipped docstring cited "schema/DEC-022 tiny-git-blob authority" as the basis for the permissive rule. DEC-022 governs compression codec selection by RAM budget and establishes nothing about content-hash satisfaction, and `schema.sql:46` states only that `files.sha256` may be null, which is a data-shape fact rather than a durability policy. DEC-022 is unaffected and remains accepted; this entry is the authority the code must cite.
- scope_boundary: This decision governs what proves durable content satisfaction. It authorises no hash-repair run against the live catalog, no restore, no change to ingestion hashing, and no PR-09 gate advance. Whether the newly failing rows are repaired, parked, or re-approved is a separate operator action.

### DEF-032: Defer reclaiming committed acceptance-evidence blobs from git history
- date: 2026-07-26 / status: active / triggered_by: PR-09 Gate-2 review — a 50,556,928-byte acceptance catalog copy (`docs/plans/evidence/b12_390_approved_fixture.sqlite`) was committed as evidence at `00ba101` and is reachable from four pushed commits / related: DEC-051, DEC-014, RFC-002, .gitignore, docs/plans/evidence/b12_390_acceptance_wall_clock.json / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, .gitignore
- decision: Stop carrying acceptance-evidence catalogs in git now, and defer reclaiming the bytes already committed. The fixture is untracked and `docs/plans/evidence/*.sqlite{,-wal,-shm}` is ignored, so no further evidence catalog can enter a commit. The committed record is the small JSON descriptor beside it, whose `source_sqlite_sha256` remains the binding identity. History is left intact: the existing blob stays reachable from `00ba101`, `3837347`, `9f77ca6`, and `768741f`.
- rationale: Removing the file from the tree cannot reclaim it. Only a rewrite of `00ba101..HEAD` plus a force-push would, and both the placement/capacity gate protocol and the PR-08 mixed-commit waiver forbid history rewriting and force-pushing while the series is open. The interim cost is bounded and smaller than it looks — the blob is roughly 8.3 MB compressed in the pack, not the 50 MB it occupies on disk. Deleting the local bytes was rejected: a re-copy of a live SQLite catalog is not byte-reproducible, so the accepted F38 fixture identity `bac9bea8…1522f` would become unverifiable. The source repository is not and must not become a git-annex repository; annex belongs to the drive fleet and the central library map repo, and model bytes are already excluded from git on that basis.
- impact: Every clone continues to fetch roughly 8.3 MB of dead weight until a rewrite happens. The B12 acceptance harness currently only *consumes* a fixture path — `recompute_fixture_identity`, `_build_full_capture_callable`, and `measure_executor_refresh_boundaries` all require the file to exist, and `_reject_synthetic_org_m_fixture` deliberately refuses synthetic data at acceptance scale. The harness therefore needs a documented way to locate or rebuild the fixture from an operator-configured runtime catalog before the file can be treated as disposable; that is PR-10 work under DEC-052, not carried by this entry.
- revisit_when: the placement/capacity series has merged and no open PR depends on `fix/placement-capacity-*` history, so a rewrite of the affected range can be performed under explicit operator authorisation; or evidence blobs recur despite the ignore rule. Evaluate git-LFS for evidence artifacts at that point — the descriptor-plus-external-bytes pattern adopted here may make LFS unnecessary.

### DEC-052: Acceptance-evidence identity is the canonical content hashes, not the container's byte hash
- date: 2026-07-26 / status: accepted; implemented at 82e3f90 (PR-10 Gate 2) / triggered_by: PR-09 Gate-2 review — the B12 harness recorded a WAL-mode SQLite container's byte hash as the binding acceptance identity while holding read-write handles on that same file / related: DEF-032, DEC-051, INC-017, DIS-002, RFC-002, modelark/execution_benchmark.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, modelark/execution_benchmark.py
- decision: (1) The binding identity of an acceptance-evidence catalog is its canonical *content* identity — `prepared_canonical_input_hash`, `prepared_projection_hash`, and the recorded row counts — all derived from ordered row values. A replacement copy of the same logical catalog is a valid artifact. (2) `source_sqlite_sha256` is retained as provenance only: it records which physical file was measured, is logged whenever it changes, and is never treated as authority or as an acceptance gate. (3) A measurement never holds a write handle on its own evidence: acceptance code opens evidence catalogs read-only, or operates on a copy when it must exercise a write-capable code path.
- rationale: A SQLite file is a mutable container whose byte layout can change while every row inside is identical — WAL checkpoint on close, freelist reuse, page reordering, or a `PRAGMA user_version` write all suffice. Pinning acceptance to that byte hash therefore pins layout rather than content, and can be invalidated by the act of measuring. The content hashes already computed by `recompute_fixture_identity` are invariant to container layout and survive a re-copy, which is precisely what DEF-032 requires now that evidence catalogs are no longer committed. Sub-decision (3) closes a live exposure: `measure_executor_refresh_boundaries` opens the artifact read-write and runs the real `fill._drain_projection` against it with no copy, so an executor write path is one refactor away from mutating the evidence it measures.
- impact: The accepted F38 evidence basis changes from file hash to content hashes; this strengthens the claim rather than weakening it, but it amends what was accepted at Gate 2 for fixture identity and must be restated in the handback rather than assumed. All `sqlite3.connect` sites in `modelark/execution_benchmark.py` become read-only or copy-first. Evidence JSON keeps both hash families with their roles made explicit.
- scope_boundary: This governs the identity of evidence *artifacts*. It changes nothing about model-file content hashing — `files.sha256`, `archived.orig_sha256`, ingestion hashing under INC-017, and the Fill satisfaction rule in DEC-051 are untouched. It authorises no gate advance.

### DEC-053: `archived` records how each original-byte digest was established
- date: 2026-07-26 / status: accepted; production frozen at `0d723c1028edd100feb6c5b815a842c8f2b5691a` / triggered_by: PR-09 Gate-2 review — DEC-051 makes durability decisions from `archived.orig_sha256` while the schema records nothing about how that digest was established, and `hash_repair._validate_candidate` already computes an `archive-head-blob` evidence tag that its `UPDATE` discards / related: DEC-051, DEC-054, DEC-055, INC-017, DIS-002, RFC-001, modelark/core/schema.sql, modelark/hash_repair.py, DEF-034 / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Add `archived.orig_sha256_provenance` under a backup-first migration matching the v6 pattern (version number follows whichever migration actually lands the column), CHECK-constrained to `hub_confirmed`, `ingestion_computed`, `annex_key`, `archive-head-blob`, `legacy_unknown`, or NULL. Meanings, strongest first: `hub_confirmed` — Hugging Face published a digest and the ingested bytes matched it; `ingestion_computed` — we hashed the original bytes at download and no Hub digest existed; `annex_key` — derived by `archive_hash.annex_sha256()` from a `SHA256`/`SHA256E` key on a raw stored copy, equal in strength to `ingestion_computed` but recovered from an existing record rather than freshly measured; `archive-head-blob` — `repair-hashes` proved the stored bytes match the blob at the same path in the archive's committed `HEAD`; `legacy_unknown` — a pre-provenance digest whose origin cannot be reconstructed; NULL — no digest, and therefore not durable. Reuse the literal string `archive-head-blob`, which is already the only evidence vocabulary in the package. Constrain the column at creation: `derivation_mode` shipped as an unconstrained `VARCHAR`, and PR-09 had to delete the permissiveness that allowed.
- rationale: The product deliberately keeps remote-header, ingestion, copy, and physical-verification evidence distinct, and INC-017's own safety boundary states that a Git-proven repair shows the bytes match our committed ingestion record rather than anything Hugging Face published. Once DEC-051 decides durability from a digest, a schema that cannot distinguish a Hub-confirmed digest from a Git-proven repair has collapsed exactly the evidence levels the project refuses to collapse. Digest provenance is a separate axis from physical verification: `verified_at` and the verifications table continue to answer "were these bytes checked on this drive", and neither substitutes for the other.
- impact: The v7 migration must not invent provenance. A non-null digest equal to `files.sha256` backfills to `hub_confirmed`; a non-null digest with no Hub digest available to compare backfills to `legacy_unknown`; a digest that *disagrees* with `files.sha256` is an incident and the migration refuses rather than guessing. Measured on the operator-approved acceptance catalog: of 5,567 archived rows, 3,252 backfill as `hub_confirmed`, 1,193 as `legacy_unknown`, 1,122 are NULL, and 0 disagree. The replica mirror propagates the source row's provenance verbatim — annex verifies the transfer by key, so a `hub_confirmed` digest is still genuinely Hub-confirmed on the replica, and inventing a separate "mirrored" level would destroy the upstream provenance rather than record it.
- scope_boundary: Adds a column and a backfill. It changes no satisfaction rule, authorises no repair run (DEC-054 governs that), and authorises no gate advance.

### DEC-054: Hash repair may run automatically per drive, without per-run operator approval
- date: 2026-07-26 / status: accepted; production frozen at `0d723c1028edd100feb6c5b815a842c8f2b5691a` / triggered_by: operator — existing installs must not be forced to re-fetch every unhashed file in order to satisfy DEC-055 / related: INC-017, DEC-050, DEC-053, DEC-055, DIS-002, modelark/hash_repair.py, modelark/archive_hash.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Amend INC-017's safety boundary for **repair only**. Hash repair may fire automatically when it detects it has not yet run, in three tiers, cheapest first: (1) `annex_key` — derive the digest from a `SHA256`/`SHA256E` key on a raw stored copy via `archive_hash.expected_sha256`; requires no file I/O and no mounted drive; (2) `archive-head-blob` — the existing `repair-hashes` path against a mounted archive, retaining every guard in `_validate_candidate`; (3) re-fetch — last resort, operator-visible, never silent. Completion is tracked **per drive and never globally**: a drive that is absent leaves its work outstanding rather than recorded as done, and no partial run may mark the repair complete. Repair state lives in its own record, not in the `user_version` ladder. Repair never overwrites an existing non-null digest, and any digest disagreeing with `files.sha256` halts the run and surfaces rather than being repaired over.
- rationale: INC-017 required operator approval because the first real repair ran against a live migrated catalog with no precedent; the guards it then built — compressed refused, stored path must equal original path, path must resolve inside the mounted archive, Git `HEAD` blob OID must match, sizes must agree — are strong enough that a bounded automatic run is safe, and tier 1 touches no files at all. The alternative is worse for existing installs: under DEC-055 an unrepaired null digest means re-downloading otherwise-durable files. A data repair across removable media is not a DDL migration, so it must not ride the schema-version ladder: DEC-050 makes an absent drive an ordinary state, and a migration that marked itself complete after running with drives unmounted would skip them permanently and invisibly.
- impact: In the acceptance catalog tier 1 alone covers all 1,122 unhashed rows — every one is `SHA256E` with `compressed=0` — so no drive needs mounting to clear them there. The live fleet may hold `WORM`/`URL`/`MD5` keys or unhashed compressed rows, so tiers 2 and 3 remain required and their coverage must be measured, not assumed. Repair writes `orig_sha256_provenance` per DEC-053 according to the tier that established the digest.
- 2026-08-03 UPDATE — **activation supersession (DEC-060).** The historical decision text above still records the original "may fire automatically" intent, but **DEC-060 supersedes activation**: the accepted frozen implementation at `0d723c1` exposes repair only via an **explicit maintenance command**, with an optional operator-visible Fill preflight remaining separately gated. Generic `db.connect()` and portal startup never begin repair. Readers must not infer that automatic-on-connect activation was implemented at the freeze tip.
- scope_boundary: This amends the repair half of INC-017's boundary only. **Restore still requires explicit operator approval**, and nothing here authorises a restore, a Fill run, or a gate advance.

### DEC-055: Content satisfaction resolves the digest through the shared restore-evidence rule
- date: 2026-07-26 / status: accepted; implementation bound to PR-10 / supersedes: DEC-051 / triggered_by: PR-09 Gate-2 review — `archive_hash.expected_sha256` is documented as the one restore-evidence rule shared by restore, verification, and legacy repair, and already derives a digest from a raw copy's annex key, but DEC-051 was written against the raw `archived.orig_sha256` column / related: DEC-051, DEC-053, DEC-054, INC-017, DIS-002, modelark/fill.py, modelark/archive_hash.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, modelark/fill.py
- decision: Keep DEC-051's rule and change only how the archived digest is obtained. For each approved file: when the approved `proposal_files.orig_sha256` is present, the stored copy's resolved digest must equal it; when the approved hash is absent, satisfaction requires that a digest is resolvable for the stored copy; when no digest is resolvable on either side, the file is neither satisfied nor source-ready and fails closed. "Resolvable" means `archive_hash.expected_sha256(catalog_sha=…, orig_sha256=…, compressed=…, annex_key=…)` returns a digest — so a raw stored copy carrying a `SHA256`/`SHA256E` annex key resolves even when `archived.orig_sha256` is null. A compressed copy's annex key names the compressed bytes and certifies nothing about the original, so compressed copies with no recorded digest continue to fail closed. The rule remains identical on the replica source and target sides.
- rationale: DEC-051 read a column while the codebase already had a canonical accessor for the same question, so Fill was stricter than restore and verification on identical evidence and parked work that the product could already prove. Routing through the shared rule removes a needless divergence and makes Fill, restore, and verification agree on what constitutes a known original-byte digest. This is the same intent as DEC-051, not a relaxation: presence of an archive row still never proves durability.
- impact: In the acceptance catalog this takes the 265 target rows that DEC-051 fails closed — all on `drive-00`, 123.6 MiB, all raw metadata files — and the 497 replica-source holds to approximately zero, because all 1,122 unhashed rows are `SHA256E` with `compressed=0`. PR-09 shipped and was accepted at Gate 2 implementing DEC-051; PR-10 moves `fill._archive_content_satisfies` onto this entry, and its regressions must add the annex-key-derived case on both the source and target sides alongside the existing four combinations.
- scope_boundary: Changes how a digest is resolved for satisfaction. It authorises no repair run, no provenance backfill, and no gate advance.

### INC-023: Approved-plan projection evaluates one fabricated file per requirement
- date: 2026-07-27 / status: accepted at 9394dbf / triggered_by: PR-10 Gate-0 helper inventory — the implementer reported `execution_projection._file_satisfied` as a second divergence on identical evidence, and reviewer investigation of the same block found three further defects / related: DEC-049, DEC-051, DEC-055, DEC-057, INC-024, INC-025, INC-027, RFC-002, modelark/execution_projection.py, modelark/execution_session.py, modelark/fill.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: An approved multi-file requirement can shrink out of the execution projection when only some of its files are durably present, dropping the remaining work. On the operator-approved 390-repository catalog all 429 executable requirements are multi-file (~19 files each), 47 are partially present on their target drive, and 170 files sit in those partially-present requirements.
- root_cause: `modelark/execution_projection.py:293-299` initialises `rfilename` to a fabricated `"model.safetensors"` and `expected_sha` to a fabricated `"1" * 64`, then overwrites both once per matching proposal file, so only the last file of a requirement is evaluated. `_file_satisfied` (lines 59-66) then returns True whenever `expected_sha` is set and the archive digest is null, and line 304 defaults `durable` to a fabricated 100 bytes. `fill._projection_work_units` evaluates every file of a requirement and accumulates `missing` correctly, so the two subsystems disagree on identical evidence.
- blast_radius: The projection is in the production path — imported by `fill.py:483` and `execution_session.py:11`. Because DEC-049 executes approved placement without re-solving, a requirement wrongly shrunk at projection time is not recovered later. The null-digest branch is not currently realised: rows with a known expected hash against a null archive digest number 0, and no null-digest archived row exceeds 100 MB, so no model weight is presently waved through on absent identity.
- why_not_caught_earlier: Review attention went to `fill`, which is where satisfaction was assumed to be decided. The projection was greped past twice without being read. The PR-10 Gate-0 helper-inventory step — added after DEC-051 was authored against a raw column while a shared accessor already existed — is what surfaced it, on its first use.
