- planned_remediation: Deliberately not folded into PR-10, whose scope is closed. Requires evaluating every approved file per requirement, removing all three fabricated defaults, and resolving digests through `archive_hash.expected_sha256` so the projection agrees with fill, restore, verification, and repair. Needs its own gate cycle with contracts; scheduling is an operator decision and may outrank PR-11's provenance work.
- 2026-07-27 UPDATE — blast radius corrected, remediation order reversed, and the `symptom` and `planned_remediation` above superseded by this line: The 47 partially-present executable requirements are **complete** under `archive_manifest.acquisition_policy()`. Zero policy-planned files are absent on any of them, under the default acquisition policy, `acquisition_policy(allow_pickle=True)`, and `recovery_policy()`, and no `ArchivePolicyError` is raised for any of the 47 or for any of the 390 selected repositories. Classified by the catalog `format` that `archive_manifest._select` actually gates on, the 170 absent `proposal_files` rows are 77 `onnx`, 51 `other`, 36 `pytorch`, and 6 `gguf`, with **zero** `aux` — every one a format the policy never selects for its repository, not an interrupted download. The single-file shrink therefore loses **no acquisition-planned work** on this catalog, and shrinking those 47 is the correct durable outcome. That is the only sense in which this defect is latent. The wider file-authority defect it masks is **active**: see INC-024 for approval-time capacity over-reservation, baseline misclassification, and 35 wrongly stalled replica placements, and INC-025 for the executor seam that independently prevents the surviving work from being fetched at all. The `planned_remediation` above is also unsafe as written: evaluating every approved file per requirement would align the projection with `fill._projection_work_units`, which already emits a work unit for all 47 whose `missing` set intersects the acquisition-planned set in **zero** files, so the fix would surface non-convergence on files `fetch.plan()` never returns. **INC-023 must not be remediated before INC-024.** Established by implementer assertion review of 2026-07-27 (A1–A6 confirmed), independently reproduced twice by the reviewer, and re-confirmed by the implementer on this cycle; an intermediate reviewer escalation to "187.8 GiB of approved work dropped" was wrong and never entered this ledger — that figure is Σ `size_bytes` of the 170 policy-excluded rows (201,650,421,030 bytes), not lost planned archives.
- 2026-07-31 UPDATE — **Gate 2 accepted / production frozen at `9394dbfa5ef0f5c99c4520ab864c3f2c91af2395`.** Gate-1 contracts initially landed at `d3b4b960a1f871a188b47b623aca05f9ce855c55`; Gate-1 remediation accepted at `b563f28badf79585db227c61b7cb1bf3038c1936`; Gate-2 production accepted with no Gate-2 remediation required. **Frozen INC-023 production tip: `9394dbf`** — no further INC-023 production without new authorization. Guarantees at that tip: (1) frozen `proposal.files` grouped once per executable `requirement_id`; missing group → `APPROVED_INPUT_CHANGED` / `missing_proposal_file_authority`; rows without usable `rfilename` → `empty_proposal_file_authority`; no fabricated `model.safetensors` / `"1"*64` / file defaults; (2) every usable approved filename evaluated for target content satisfaction; digests resolve through `archive_hash.expected_sha256(catalog_sha=None, orig_sha256=…, compressed=bool(…), annex_key=…)` (approved digest present → resolved must exist and match case-insensitively; approved absent → non-empty resolved required; missing row or unresolvable → unsatisfied); no live catalog/file policy reopen for satisfaction; (3) `_catalog_projection_bundle` archived envelope includes `compressed` and `annex_key` alongside `orig_sha256` / `stored_bytes` / `orig_bytes`; (4) missing/null `guaranteed_durable` → `APPROVAL_PROJECTION_VIOLATION` / `missing_guaranteed_durable`; explicit zero remains exact and is never coerced via `or 100` or replaced/derived from proposal file sizes; (5) stored overrun runs before shrink, sums `stored_bytes` of approved filenames on the task target only, uses the exact task durable, excludes unapproved archive rows, applies only when durable > 0, refuses `APPROVED_PLACEMENT_NO_LONGER_FEASIBLE` with `reason=stored_bytes_overrun` and `stored=<exact approved aggregate>`; compression-ratio path preserved; (6) shrink only when every approved file is content-satisfied on target (partial remains order-independently); (7) replica source readiness evaluates every approved file on source; primary completion uses the primary requirement’s own frozen file group and target; incomplete source + unfinished primary → `waiting_dependency`; incomplete source + completed/no unfinished primary → `APPROVAL_PROJECTION_VIOLATION` / `source_not_ready` with exact `source` and `repo`; fully satisfied replica target shrinks before source-readiness evaluation. Evidence at freeze tip: `tests/test_inc023_gate1_contracts.py` 16 passed; full pytest excluding portal E2E 684 passed, 5 warnings; `ruff check modelark tests/test_inc023_gate1_contracts.py` clean; `git diff --check` clean. **Boundaries preserved:** INC-027 remains open and was not implemented — baseline certificate self-copy / any-row archival evidence remains for the DEC-053 / DEC-054 provenance and baseline-evidence cycle; no INC-024, INC-025, blocked-selection, schema, migration, Fill, proposal, fetch, or archive-policy reopening by this closeout; DEF-035 remains unallocated; PR #55 remains draft and unmerged. Next series work after this closeout is DEC-053 / DEC-054 plus the DEF-034 `derivation_mode` CHECK — not started by this bookkeeping commit.

### INC-024: Approved proposals record and charge catalog files the archive policy will never fetch
- date: 2026-07-27 / status: accepted at 5bf0f73 / triggered_by: PR-10 Gate-0 helper inventory and the 2026-07-27 assertion review — investigating INC-023's blast radius showed the projection was masking a proposal-construction defect / related: INC-023, INC-025, DEC-049, DEC-051, DEC-055, DEC-056, RFC-002, modelark/proposal.py, modelark/archive_manifest.py, modelark/fill.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: An approved proposal treats the entire catalog file list as the planned set, in both its file rows and its capacity arithmetic. `proposal_files` carries a row for every catalog file in the repository, including formats the archive policy never selects, each stamped `role='missing'` and `storage_action='compress'` as though it were outstanding work; and each executable task's durable byte charge is the sum of every catalog row's `size_bytes`. For `Alibaba-NLP/gte-base-en-v1.5`: catalog `files` 18, `proposal_files` 18 per requirement, `acquisition_policy` plans 11, archived on `drive-00` 11 — the seven `onnx/*` rows are permanently unfetchable work items.
- root_cause: Archive eligibility is derived on demand and never stored — `archive_manifest._select` (`modelark/archive_manifest.py:70-126`) chooses whole-repo weight format by precedence (safetensors, else GGUF, else opted-in pickle) plus all `aux`, so it cannot be expressed as a per-file column. Four reads on the proposal path take `files` raw instead of calling it: `_append_missing_files` (`modelark/proposal.py:311-324`) appends every catalog row as `role='missing'`/`storage_action='compress'`; `_manifest_hash` (`:156-162`) hashes the same unfiltered list into `proposal_tasks.full_manifest_hash`, which `execution_projection.project_pure` revalidates per task for drift, so narrowing one without the other changes drift semantics; `_repo_size` (`:304-308`) sums every row's `size_bytes` into `guaranteed_durable` and `expected_durable`; and `_complete_archived_plan_drives` (`:263-301`) requires **every** catalog row to have a content-matching `archived` row before a drive counts as a durable baseline, so a repository holding any policy-excluded file can never be recognised as satisfied. `_repo_workspace_peak` (`:362-385`) is the one read on this path that does filter (`format='safetensors'`), which shows the distinction was understood where transient workspace was concerned and not where durable size and durability class were.
- blast_radius: Measured on the operator-approved 390-repository acceptance catalog, approved proposal `824fce33`, 429 executable and 65 baseline-satisfied tasks. **File authority:** 9,520 `proposal_files` rows across the 429 executable requirements, resolving to 8,128 distinct `(repo_id, rfilename)` pairs, which equals the catalog `files` count on those repositories — the full per-requirement catalog dump, not the acquisition-planned subset; baseline requirements carry no file rows. **Non-convergence:** of the 429, 47 are partially present on target and 382 fully absent, and none fully present under `proposal_files`; for the 47, `fill._projection_work_units` emits a unit whose `missing` set intersects the acquisition-planned set in zero files, and INC-023's single-file shrink currently removes exactly those 47 before fill sees them, whereas on the 382 fully-absent requirements INC-023 masks nothing — the two defects do not cancel globally. **Replica readiness:** `fill._source_files_content_ready` requires content satisfaction for every `proposal_files` row on the source drive, so policy-excluded rows that are never archived anywhere hold replicas open — 29 of 104 replica tasks are source-ready under the recorded wide list against 64 under the acquisition-planned subset, leaving **35 replica placements permanently at `waiting_dependency`**. **Capacity:** Σ `guaranteed_durable` over the 429 executable tasks is 17,128,131,340,975 bytes (15.58 TiB) against a policy-correct 12,306,142,285,959 bytes (11.19 TiB) — **4,821,989,055,016 bytes, 4.39 TiB, 28.2% of the approved reservation is bytes the policy will never fetch**. **Baseline misclassification:** all **47 of 47** partials have their acquisition-planned set present and content-matching under `proposal._archived_matches_manifest` on an active plan-member drive, so a policy-filtered completeness check would class them `baseline_satisfied` with no fetch and no charge; they are instead `executable` and charged 3,719,535,531,900 bytes (3.38 TiB) for already-durable work. **Placement, not only accounting:** against safety-adjusted admissible free, `drive-00` is charged 4.23 TiB of 4.23 TiB and `drive-01` 7.07 TiB of 7.07 TiB — 100.0% each, leaving 0.3 GiB and 0.2 GiB residual — while their policy-correct charges are 2.87 TiB and 4.37 TiB; because `_build_assignment` depletes `remaining[label]` by `guaranteed_durable`, phantom bytes filled the first two drives to the byte and displaced real work onto `drive-02`, `drive-03`, and `drive-06`. Gate B reported FEASIBLE with effectively zero headroom on two drives, so a slightly fuller fleet would have reported INFEASIBLE on phantom bytes. PR-10 introduced none of this — `modelark/proposal.py` is absent from PR-10's diff — so under the series scope-freeze rule it does not block PR-10's Gate 2; the whole placement subsystem is new in this series and has never been on `origin/main`, so this is a defect in unshipped code rather than a regression against a released path.
- why_not_caught_earlier: Archive eligibility is a shared derivation that consumers must remember to call, and nothing prevents a raw read of `files`. This is the third instance of that shape: `fill` bypassed `archive_hash.expected_sha256` (DEC-051, closed by DEC-055), `execution_projection._file_satisfied` still bypasses it, and the proposal path bypasses `archive_manifest.manifest_for_repo` in four separate places. Each was found by reading the code, not by a test. The capacity half is additionally invisible to every existing assertion because `joint_capacity_shortfall` is verified against `guaranteed_durable` as supplied and never against an independently derived planned size, so no test can observe that the charge is wider than the plan.
- planned_remediation: Narrow file authority and durable accounting to the acquisition-planned set at all four reads — record the planned subset at the single write site, base `guaranteed_durable`/`expected_durable` on planned size, and make baseline completeness require the planned set rather than the whole catalog — and decide deliberately what `proposal_tasks.full_manifest_hash` covers, since it is revalidated per task for drift and narrowing one side alone changes drift semantics silently. Existing approved proposals carry the wide set, so the migration path — re-derive, or tolerate both shapes for a release — is part of the decision, as is whether already-approved capacity charges are restated or left as historical record. Must land **before** INC-023, and coordinate with INC-025, whose remediation must not be allowed to fetch the excluded rows this entry describes. Broader enforcement — preventing raw `files` access outside the manifest module — is explicitly out of scope by operator decision.

### INC-025: Approved-projection fetch cannot execute — the drain hands fetch an untyped, policy-unfiltered manifest
- date: 2026-07-27 / status: accepted at 595c393 / triggered_by: PR-10 reviewer investigation of INC-024's blast radius — following the drain past `fetch.plan()` showed the executor never calls it / related: INC-023, INC-024, INC-004, DEC-049, DEC-055, RFC-002, modelark/fill.py, modelark/fetch.py, modelark/archive_manifest.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, modelark/fill.py
- symptom: On `fix/placement-capacity-hardening` an approved fill cannot archive any bytes through the projection drain. Every FETCH work unit raises `AttributeError: 'types.SimpleNamespace' object has no attribute 'as_fetch_record'` inside `fetch_model` before any download begins. `fetch.run` isolates it per repository as a generic failure (`modelark/fetch.py:1148`) and records `outcome='error'` in `fetch_events` with the exception text; the drain re-derives the same units, retries the same repository, and terminalizes as `FETCH_TASK_FAILED` once a repository reaches `_MAX_TASK_ATTEMPTS` (2) at `modelark/fill.py:1030-1039`. It fails closed and loudly — no partial writes, no silent success, no unbounded loop — but it never progresses.
- root_cause: `fill._drain_projection` (`modelark/fill.py:936-941`) builds the `task_manifests` override from `u.file_rows`, which `_projection_work_units` constructs as plain `SimpleNamespace(rfilename, size_bytes, sha256, format, quant)` (`:714-717`) and which carries no `storage_action`. `fetch.run` forwards it as `manifest=` (`modelark/fetch.py:1066-1067`), and `fetch_model` then does two incompatible things with it: at `:762` it takes the override **in place of** `archive_manifest.manifest_for_repo`, so the acquisition policy is not consulted at all for that task, and at `:765` it calls `item.as_fetch_record()`, which is defined only on `archive_manifest.ManifestFile` (`modelark/archive_manifest.py:40-49`). The parameter is already declared `Sequence[archive_manifest.ManifestFile]` at `modelark/fetch.py:943`; nothing enforces the annotation. Introduced by `34829db` ("PR-09 Gate-2: close findings 24-27 production authority"): the hard cut removed the reconcile snapshot from `fill.execute`, and the prior construction — `snapshot.graph.manifests[task.repo_id]` filtered by `task.budget.missing_files`, which was both correctly typed and policy-filtered — was replaced by the projection's own rows. The intent was RFC-002 file authority (do not reopen mutable catalog `files`); the effect was to drop the type contract and the policy gate together.
- blast_radius: 278 of the 429 approved executable requirements are FETCH units that survive the INC-023 projection shrink and reach this path; the executable set is 325 FETCH plus 104 REPLICATE, all 47 partially-present requirements are FETCH and all 47 shrink out before fetch, and the 104 REPLICATE units execute through `fetch.run_replica_tasks` and never touch this seam — they are separately stalled for a different reason recorded in INC-024. The defect is confined to the series integration branch: `34829db` is on `fix/placement-capacity-hardening` and not on `origin/main`, whose `fill.py` still builds manifests from `snapshot.graph.manifests` and contains no `_projection_work_units`. Merging `hardening` into `main` would therefore replace a working fetch path with a non-progressing one, which makes this a **regression** against shipped behaviour rather than a defect in new code, and the first hazard on that merge. PR-10 did not introduce it — `git diff a2c3707..82e3f90 -- modelark/fill.py` touches only the DEC-055 satisfaction helpers around lines 544, 578, and 686 — so under the series scope-freeze rule it does not block PR-10's Gate 2, whose acceptance at `82e3f90` stands.
- why_not_caught_earlier: The seam is verified on both sides and never across the join. Every test that reaches it monkeypatches it away — `tests/test_replan.py`, `tests/test_gate2_hardcut_authority.py`, `tests/test_gate2_findings_33_38.py`, and `tests/test_transport_fence.py` all patch `fetch.run` or `fetch.fetch_model` — while every test that exercises the real `fetch_model` with a `manifest=` argument hand-constructs genuine `ManifestFile` objects (`tests/test_fetch_resume.py:73`, `tests/test_hash_repair.py:257`, `:288`, `:318`, `tests/test_transport_fence.py:923`). So 618 passing tests and a green e2e job say nothing about the only production caller, and no type checker runs over the declared signature. The same shape as INC-024's finding one level deeper: there the shared derivation was merely not called, here the type that would have carried it was discarded too.
- planned_remediation: Restore a typed, policy-filtered manifest at the drain seam — derive `ManifestFile` values through `archive_manifest` for the approved repository and intersect them with the unit's `missing_files`, as the pre-`34829db` construction did — and add a contract that crosses the seam with a real `fetch_model` and a drain-produced manifest so both the type and the policy gate are asserted at the join rather than on either side of it. **Adapting only the call shape is not an acceptable fix:** making the raw `file_rows` satisfy `as_fetch_record` while the override still bypasses `acquisition_policy` would convert a fail-closed drain into one that downloads and archives policy-excluded formats, which is worse than the present state. Because `proposal_files` is the RFC-002 file authority and currently holds the unfiltered catalog list (INC-024), either narrow that recorded set first or filter at this seam and record why the two authorities differ until INC-024 lands.
- 2026-07-30 UPDATE — Gate 1 accepted at `a260585907525cee52de602831ca642b05a69f57` (contracts prove real drain calls `_fetch_task_manifests` and exact typed refusals). Gate 0 design correction stands for **approved** execution: convert approved missing rows to `ManifestFile` at the FETCH drain; **never** re-read live `manifest_for_repo`/catalog/policy on the approved `proposal_files` path. Gate 2 production implements that design in `modelark/fill.py` (`_projection_work_units` preserves frozen `storage_action`; `_fetch_task_manifests` builds typed manifests; drain wires a single helper call). Review arrangement for this Gate 2: Grok implements; Codex dispositions the gate; Opus whole-PR review once #55 is otherwise complete; operator alone merges; Greptile not required while this arrangement remains active. PR #55 remains draft and is not mergeable by this authorization alone.
- 2026-07-30 UPDATE — Gate-2 remediation (non-approval characterization): the non-approval branch of `_projection_work_units` (`require_proposal_files` false, no proposal-file authority) must call the canonical `archive_manifest.manifest_for_repo` and carry its returned `ManifestFile` fields into `file_specs` — not inspect `FLOAT_QUANTS`, not classify every raw catalog row, and not independently recreate selection/action policy. Live catalog/policy is permitted **only** on that characterization branch; approved execution remains frozen-authority-only and never rereads live policy. Regression: `tests/test_inc025_gate2_remediation.py` (fails on `1be2150`, green after fix). Gate 2 remains under reviewer review.
- 2026-07-30 UPDATE — **Gate 2 accepted at `595c393a2ee2076090b8a2f8b4dc3b37baa26ab6`.** Codex accepted INC-025 Gate 2 after Gate-2 production (`1be2150`) and characterization remediation (`595c393`). Frozen production tip for INC-025: **`595c393`** — no further INC-025 production without new authorization. Guarantees at that tip: (1) approved path — frozen `proposal_files` sole file authority; `storage_action` preserved through `_projection_work_units`; `_fetch_task_manifests` resolves every missing filename to exactly one frozen approved row as genuine `archive_manifest.ManifestFile`; missing/duplicate/invalid-`storage_action` fail closed with exact `APPROVED_INPUT_CHANGED` refusals; `_drain_projection` calls the helper once and passes its exact return to `fetch.run`; no live catalog/policy reread; no empty-intersection fallback to all unit rows; (2) non-approval characterization — canonical `manifest_for_repo` only; no independent FLOAT_QUANTS/raw-catalog policy; `ArchivePolicyError` fail-closed; (3) boundaries — `fetch.py`, `archive_manifest.py`, schema/migrations, replica execution, INC-023, and blocked-selection behavior unchanged by INC-025. Evidence: focused contracts `tests/test_inc025_gate1_contracts.py` + `tests/test_inc025_gate2_remediation.py` (12 passed); full suite 646 passed, 5 warnings; CI on `595c393` green for Python 3.10, 3.12, and e2e. Gate-2 remediation independently verified red on parent `1be2150` and green on `595c393`. INC-025 production is complete; next series work is blocked-selection workflow (Gate 0 inventory authorized separately).

### DEF-033: Defer the verifier's policy-error false-clean disposition
- date: 2026-07-27 / status: resolved by Gate-2 production at `87b51fd8676d4c5f6d635774588f73da9a2de817` / triggered_by: PR-10 assertion review item A6 — `verifier.reverify` reports a repository whose archive policy cannot be evaluated as complete rather than unknown / related: INC-023, INC-024, INC-017, DEC-051, DEC-053, DEC-055, DEC-060, DIS-002, modelark/verifier.py, modelark/archive_manifest.py, modelark/web/static/verify.js / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: Leave both policy-error fallbacks in `modelark/verifier.py` as they stand for now, and record them so neither is mistaken for intended semantics later. In `reverify`, `except archive_manifest.ArchivePolicyError: planned_names = set(by_name)` (`:170-171`) makes `missing = planned_names - set(by_name)` (`:173`) empty by construction, so `record_ok` can be true for a repository whose policy cannot be evaluated — a false **clean**, reporting OK where `unknown` is the honest answer. In `suspects`, the sibling fallback `required = archived_names.get(repo, set())` when a manifest is absent (`:92-95`) compares a union of names across all drives against one drive's names, so it can flag a legitimate multi-drive layout as a partial copy — a false **suspect**. One pattern, opposite failure directions. Verify and restore behaviour is unchanged in the interim.
- rationale: Neither path is exercised by the evidence in hand — 0 of the 390 selected repositories in the acceptance catalog raise `ArchivePolicyError` under `recovery_policy()` — and the adjacent comment states the permissive fallback is deliberate for legacy and foreign formats already accepted into the archive, which is a real requirement rather than an oversight. Choosing between "report OK", "report unknown", and "fail closed" is a durability-semantics decision that belongs with DEC-053's provenance vocabulary rather than being settled inside a docs-only ledger cycle: the product's standing boundary keeps remote-header, ingestion, copy, and physical-verification evidence distinct, which argues for `unknown`, but not before deciding what `unknown` obliges an operator to do.
- revisit_when: any repository in the live fleet raises `ArchivePolicyError` under `recovery_policy()`, or DEC-053's provenance column lands and gives `unknown` a recordable meaning — whichever comes first. Measure the live-fleet count before deciding; the acceptance catalog's 0 of 390 is evidence about that catalog only and says nothing about `WORM`/`URL`/`MD5` keys or unsupported foreign formats on the drives.
- 2026-07-28 UPDATE — revisit condition met: the deferral above rested on the false-clean path being unexercised, and it is exercised on the operator's live catalog. Measured read-only on the pre-migration runtime catalog (4,118 models, 101,652 files, 444 finalized selections, 2,311 archived rows), 1 of the 140 repositories holding archived rows raises `ArchivePolicyError` under `recovery_policy()`: `nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2`, 3 archived rows against 4 catalog files (3 `aux`, 1 `other`, no weights in any supported format). For that repository `reverify` sets `planned_names` to the archived names, `missing` is empty by construction, and `record_ok` is true — it reports the archive complete while a catalog file is absent and the policy cannot be evaluated at all. Exposure is bounded at one repository and 3 archived rows on that catalog; the 0-of-390 figure in the rationale above remains accurate for the acceptance fixture and was never evidence about the live fleet. The deferral **stands** on the strength of that bound, but its revisit condition is now satisfied rather than pending, so the disposition is owed at the next `verifier` or provenance cycle rather than at an indefinite later point.
- 2026-08-03 UPDATE — **resolved / accepted at `87b51fd8676d4c5f6d635774588f73da9a2de817`.** Gate-1 contracts (`tests/test_def033_gate1_contracts.py`) and Gate-2 production (`modelark/verifier.py`, `modelark/web/static/verify.js`) are reviewer-accepted. **Frozen DEF-033 production tip: `87b51fd`.** Frozen interface: (1) recovery-policy `ArchivePolicyError` is operator-visible **unknown** (`status=="unknown"`, `ok is False`, `record_ok is None`, `missing is None`, `policy_error=={"code":"ARCHIVE_POLICY_UNKNOWN","detail":str(original_exception)}`); (2) known digest disagreement, missing mounted bytes, or insufficient copies retain **`status=="failed"`** while the exact `policy_error` object and `missing is None` remain attached; digest disagreement sets `record_ok is False`, physical/insufficient without digest disagreement leave `record_ok is None`; physical PASS never upgrades policy-unknown to verified; (3) `suspects()` surfaces every `ManifestBatch.errors` repository as type exactly `"unknown"` with the original exception text in `reasons`, never as integrity/partial-copy solely from policy failure; independent integrity reasons coexist as sorted types `["integrity","unknown"]`; (4) policy-only unknown follow-ups authorize **no automatic** repair, restore, deletion, or other mutation — manual re-verify may report the typed unknown; Verify UI counts/renders `unknown` neutrally (`${unknown.length}`, mut badge) and excludes unknown-only from bulk integrity re-verify. No further DEF-033 production without new authorization. Next provenance-cycle gate: **INC-027** (baseline certificate self-copy).

### DEC-056: Approval drift is measured against the acquisition-planned set, not the whole catalog
- date: 2026-07-28 / status: accepted; implementation bound to the INC-024 remediation / triggered_by: INC-024 Gate 0 — narrowing `proposal._manifest_hash` to the policy-planned set changes what invalidates an approval, which is a policy choice rather than a side effect of the fix / related: INC-024, INC-023, INC-025, DEC-049, DEC-050, DEC-055, RFC-002, modelark/proposal.py, modelark/execution_session.py, modelark/execution_projection.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: `proposal_tasks.full_manifest_hash` binds the **acquisition-planned** file set for a repository, not its whole catalog file list. `proposal._manifest_hash` narrows internally — it calls the archive policy itself rather than accepting an injected file list — so every consumer narrows together: the two draft write sites, the approve-time re-validation in `validate_exact_assignment`, and the projection comparison input built in `execution_session`. An optional pre-computed planned set may be passed for efficiency, never as the mechanism. Consequently a catalog change **outside** the planned set — Hugging Face publishing a new ONNX export, adding an `.h5` mirror, a policy-excluded file changing size — no longer invalidates an existing approval; only a change to the planned set's identity does.
- rationale: A hash over the whole catalog answers "did anything about this repository change" when an approval needs "did the work I approved change" — INC-024 records the first form as the defect. Narrowing inside the single function is required rather than stylistic: `_manifest_hash` has consumers on both sides of the drift comparison, so any consumer left computing the wide hash would report permanent spurious drift and refuse every approval. The alternative — keep the wide hash and accept that it denotes something other than the planned file list — was rejected because it leaves two definitions of the approved set inside one artifact, which is precisely the divergence class DEC-051, DEC-055, and INC-024 each record.
- impact: Existing approved proposals hold wide hashes, so once this lands `execution_projection.project_pure` refuses them `APPROVED_INPUT_CHANGED` with reason `full_manifest_hash`, and `validate_exact_assignment` refuses stale wide drafts at approve time. That is a typed fail-closed rather than silent misexecution, and the remedy is re-preview and re-approve: `approve()` supersedes prior approvals and `proposal_files` is keyed per `proposal_id`, so a fresh approval regenerates narrow rows and leaves the wide set as superseded history. **No data migration is required.** Drift detection becomes deliberately less sensitive; the narrower signal is the one an approval contract needs.
- scope_boundary: Governs what `full_manifest_hash` covers and how it is computed. It authorises no executor change (INC-025 stays open), no projection change (INC-023 stays open), no schema migration, and no gate advance.

### DEF-CATALOG-004: Park the archive-policy-unarchivable selections pending pickle support
- date: 2026-07-28 / status: active / triggered_by: INC-024 Gate 0 — measuring the operator's live selection against the archive policy found 54 finalized repositories that cannot produce a manifest, which under the INC-024 remediation make the whole draft INFEASIBLE / related: INC-024, DEC-050, DEC-056, modelark/archive_manifest.py, wishlist.yaml / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- observation: Of 444 finalized selections on the pre-migration runtime catalog, 54 raise `ArchivePolicyError` under `acquisition_policy()` with `exclude.pickle_only: true` — 50 because their only usable weights are pickle, and 4 because they carry no weights in any supported format at all (`arcee-ai/Trinity-Tokenizer`, `NousResearch/ByteDance-Seed-OSS-36B-Alternate-Tokenizer`, `nvidia/parakeet-tdt-0.6b-v2`, `HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM-360M-Instruct-ONNX-fp16`). 30 of the 54 already hold archived `aux` bytes on drives from an earlier policy era; nothing in the product drops archived rows or annex keys, and the one `files` deletion path refuses rows having a matching `archived` or `replicas` row, so those bytes stay. Setting `exclude.pickle_only: false` would reduce the 54 to the same 4; the operator has declined for now on the grounds that the project is not ready to store pickle weights.
- deferral: The 50 pickle-only repositories are parked, not pruned — they leave the finalized selection so planning can proceed, and are re-selected when pickle support lands. The 4 carrying no supported weights are a prune decision rather than a deferral: the archive policy has no representation for an aux-only artifact, and the operator has accepted that refusing them is correct rather than a gap to close. No archived bytes are dropped for either group, and the selection stands at the remainder in the interim.
- revisit_when: pickle weight support ships — whether as `exclude.pickle_only: false` becoming viable or as an explicit inert-pickle storage path — at which point re-select the 50 and re-preview; or the archive policy gains a representation for aux-only artifacts, at which point the 4 are reconsidered on their own merits.
- 2026-07-28 UPDATE — numbering: this entry should have been DEF-CATALOG-003. The reviewer derived the next free id from a count that included the `### DEF-CATALOG-###` template line in this file's header, so entries 001 and 002 were read as three. DEF-CATALOG-003 was never allocated and never will be; the gap is a reviewer counting error, not a missing, withdrawn, or redacted entry. Recorded so a later reader does not search for it.

### INC-026: CI runs test files as scripts, so a quarter of the suite never executes
- date: 2026-07-28 / status: remediated by DEC-057 / triggered_by: INC-024 Gate-1 implementer handback — the implementer noticed CI could report green while the Gate-1 contracts were red under pytest / related: INC-024, INC-025, DEC-052, RFC-002, .github/workflows/ci.yml / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, .github/workflows/ci.yml
- symptom: The `test` job reports success while test functions fail. `python tests/test_pr10_gate1_contracts.py` exits 0 having executed no assertion, while `pytest` on the same file executes 16. Commit `bc36aed` shows `test (3.10)`, `test (3.12)`, and `e2e` all green while 13 of that commit's 14 Gate-1 contracts fail under pytest.
- root_cause: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `for t in tests/test_*.py; do python "$t"; done` — each file as a script rather than under a collector. Executing a module defines its `def test_*` functions and never calls them. 46 of 66 test files end with an explicit `if __name__ == "__main__":` block that calls their tests by hand and so run under either invocation; the other 20 were written for pytest and have no such block, so CI defines their functions and exits 0. Measured: **161 of 632 test functions never execute in CI**.
- blast_radius: The silently-skipped set is concentrated in this series' gate contracts — `test_gate2_findings_33_38.py` (28 functions, PR-09 Gate 2), `test_pr10_gate1_contracts.py` (16, PR-10 Gate 1), `test_inc024_gate1_contracts.py` (14), `test_capacity.py` (17), `test_reconcile.py` (13), `test_execution_projection.py` (12), `test_execution_sessions.py` (12), `test_projection_performance_contract.py` (6), `test_gate2_hardcut_authority.py` (5), `test_gate2_worker_session_lifecycle.py` (5). Every "CI green" claim recorded as gate evidence in PR-09 and PR-10 therefore did not exercise those contracts, and the acceptance recorded for PR-10 at code tip `82e3f90` cited a CI run that never called its 16 Gate-1 pins. No defect is concealed: collected properly, `pytest -q --ignore=tests/test_e2e_portal.py` reports 619 passed against the 13 expected-red INC-024 contracts, so what failed is the evidence chain rather than the code. `ci.yml` is unchanged since `origin/main` and carries the same loop there, so this predates the placement/capacity series and blocks no gate in it under the scope-freeze rule.
- why_not_caught_earlier: A green check is the least suspicious signal in a repository, and the job is named `test`. The loop is also genuinely load-bearing for the 46 script-style files, so it reads as deliberate rather than partial. This is the same "verification that cannot fail" family already recorded in this series — the F38 refresh count, the false `git diff --check` claim, and the v6 short-hash probe's `except Exception: pass` — and it is the largest instance: a whole class of evidence rather than a single assertion.
- planned_remediation: Collect the suite with `pytest`, retaining `--ignore=tests/test_e2e_portal.py` so the E2E job keeps its own invocation — that file carries a `__main__` entry point and no `def test_*` functions. Expect the job to go **red immediately and correctly** on INC-024's 13 expected-red contracts, returning green when the INC-024 remediation lands; a red badge at a tests-only gate is the intended signal. The `__main__` blocks in the other 46 files become redundant and are left in place, as removing them is unrelated cleanup. Until this lands, gate acceptances must name pytest as the measurement rather than CI.

### DEF-034: Defer the `derivation_mode` CHECK to the migration that adds DEC-053 digest provenance
- date: 2026-07-28 / status: resolved by DEC-053 production at `0d723c1` / triggered_by: PR-10 review — this constraint had been scheduled against a position in the PR queue rather than against an artifact, so re-sequencing the series left the commitment pointing at work that no longer carried the migration, recorded only in a plan document / related: DEC-053, DEC-056, INC-024, INC-025, RFC-002, modelark/core/schema.sql / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: `placement_proposals.derivation_mode` (`modelark/core/schema.sql:308`) stays an unconstrained `VARCHAR` until the backup-first migration that adds `archived.orig_sha256_provenance` under DEC-053 runs, and that migration constrains it to the RFC-002 values `optimized`, `state_truncated`, and `canonical_fallback` in the same version bump. The binding anchor is that migration, identified by the column it adds and the decision that authorises it — **not** a PR number and **not** a schema-version number. DEC-053 currently anticipates v7, but if any other migration lands first this constraint travels with the provenance column to whatever version it actually receives. No standalone schema version is created for `derivation_mode` alone.
- rationale: With the `ecfg:` read deleted in PR-09 this is audit hygiene rather than an authority bypass, so it does not justify a migration of its own, and DEC-053 already opens a version bump where a second CHECK is nearly free — DEC-053 itself records that constraining at creation is the lesson `derivation_mode` taught. Anchoring deferred work to a queue position is the failure this entry corrects: the earlier disposition was right in substance and went stale the moment the series resequenced, while its only record sat in a handback document. An anchor naming the artifact cannot go stale, and the charter's requirement that every deferral exist in the repository before the series reaches `main` is then satisfiable independently of ordering.
- revisit_when: the DEC-053 provenance migration is authored — constrain the column in that same migration; or the series reaches a merge to `main` with that migration still unwritten, in which case decide explicitly whether to ship the column unconstrained.
- 2026-08-02 UPDATE — **resolved by DEC-053 production at `0d723c1`.** The provenance migration that landed under DEC-053/054 Gate 2 constrains `placement_proposals.derivation_mode` to `optimized` / `state_truncated` / `canonical_fallback` while preserving NULL for historical rows. No further DEF-034 work remains; the freeze tip is `0d723c1028edd100feb6c5b815a842c8f2b5691a`.

### DEC-057: Gate process — scope freeze, Gate-0 inventory, pytest measurement
- date: 2026-07-29 / status: accepted / triggered_by: placement/capacity series process rules lived only in untracked plan docs; INC-026 showed gate evidence can cite a green badge while the suite never collected the contracts being accepted / related: INC-026, INC-024, INC-025, DEC-052, DEF-034, RFC-002, .github/workflows/ci.yml / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md / remediates: INC-026
- decision: Three standing gate-process rules, now ledger-bound rather than plan-doc only:
  1. **Scope freeze.** Only a regression introduced by the **current gate** blocks that gate. Every other finding — pre-existing relative to the gate, latent, or newly discovered in adjacent code — enters the decision log (INC/DEF/DEC as appropriate) and does not expand the open gate's production scope.
  2. **Gate 0 inventory.** Before defining a new rule, Gate 0 must inventory the existing helper/accessor that answers the same question and name any sibling subsystem that already decides it. Authoring a parallel path without that inventory is out of process.
  3. **Pytest measurement.** Gate acceptance is measured through the repository's real **pytest** collector (`pytest` / CI's `pytest -q --ignore=tests/test_e2e_portal.py`), not inferred from a badge, job name, or script invocation that may define `test_*` functions without calling them.
- rationale: Scope freeze is what kept INC-023/024/025/026 out of open gates and into the ledger; without it every review round re-absorbs the product. Gate 0 inventory is what found DEC-055's shared accessor, INC-023's projection divergence, and INC-024's four unfiltered reads — inventing rules mid-implementation has been the series' defect class. INC-026 is the measurement half: CI ran `python tests/test_*.py`, which only *defines* pytest-style tests, so 161 of 632 functions never executed and PR-09/PR-10 gate acceptances that cited CI green did not exercise their Gate-1 contracts. Remediation of the CI job (collect with pytest) is the mechanical fix; this entry is the process rule that makes a green badge without collection invalid as gate evidence going forward. A surviving measurement-contract weakness on a single DEC-052 pin (pre-drain typed failure still leaves bytes unchanged) is a separate semantic concern and does **not** reopen INC-026.
- impact: CI collects the full suite from `b653bfd` onward. Future gate acceptances must name pytest results (or a CI job that is itself pytest). Scope freeze and Gate-0 inventory remain mandatory on every subsequent gate in this series and on later series that adopt the same staged model. INC-026 is remediated by this entry; its CI fix is already on the tip.
- scope_boundary: Process and measurement only. Authorises no production code, no gate advance, no disposition of Greptile threads, and no change to any product rule.

### DEC-058: Blocked-selection portal authority — pure preview, reduced response, CAS dismiss
- date: 2026-07-30 / status: accepted at 7db75dd / triggered_by: blocked-selection Gate 0 inventory at bookkeeping tip 06fd604 — reviewer disposition locked read-only snapshot, reduced response, and atomic preview CAS before Gate 1 contracts / related: INC-024, DEC-049, DEC-050, DEC-057, DEF-CATALOG-004, RFC-002, modelark/proposal.py, modelark/web/plan_api.py, modelark/web/selection_api.py, modelark/web/static/fill.js / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, tests/test_blocked_selection_gate1_contracts.py, tests/test_blocked_selection_gate2_remediation.py, tests/test_e2e_portal.py
- decision:
  1. **Evidence authority.** The blocked-selection notice uses only the canonical proposal path: `proposal.preview_pure(..., mutation=("adopt_current", ()))` → optional `gate_b_refusal` single container (INC-024 Q2). `librarian.plan_view` / `/api/library/plan` remain the capacity/reconcile advisory surface and must never supply Dismiss IDs or reconstruct `gate_b_refusal`.
  2. **Reduced read-only portal preview.** `GET /api/plan/preview` → `plan_api.preview()` opens a dedicated `db.connect(read_only=True)` connection, establishes a coherent read snapshot, resolves the active plan **without** bootstrapping or writing, calls `preview_pure` exactly once, closes the connection, and returns a reduced payload: `{ok, plan_id, based_on_revision, selection_before_hash, gate_b_code, gate_b_refusal}`. `gate_b_refusal` is the exact nested container from pure preview (or null when absent). Tasks, files, and the full proposal payload are not exposed. The portal shared `data.conn()` and `data._lock` must not be used for planning on this path.
  3. **Fill-tab ownership.** The Fill tab owns the MANIFEST_POLICY notice (list escaped repo_id + reason; Dismiss + Replan). Capacity-only blockers stay on the existing plan_view advisory path; this notice handles only `gate_b_refusal.code == "MANIFEST_POLICY"`.
  4. **Dismiss CAS (complete pair).** Bound bulk removal posts exact displayed blocked IDs with `on: false` plus `expected_revision` and `expected_selection_hash` (the preview's `based_on_revision` and `selection_before_hash`). **Supplying either CAS key expresses bound intent.** Both keys must be present and non-null together: incomplete/null pair → HTTP 409 `PREVIEW_BINDINGS_REQUIRED` with exact body `{ok:false, refused:true, code:"PREVIEW_BINDINGS_REQUIRED", error:"expected_revision and expected_selection_hash must be provided together.", actions:["replan"]}` — no deletion, no revision bump; refusal returns through the guarded removal path so **FILL_SESSION_ACTIVE still takes precedence** when Fill owns the mutation gate. When the complete pair is present, both comparisons run inside the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` before any deletion; mismatch → HTTP 409 `PREVIEW_STALE` with exact body `{ok:false, refused:true, code:"PREVIEW_STALE", error:"Selection changed since this preview. Replan before dismissing.", evidence:{current_revision, based_on_revision, selection_changed}, actions:["replan"]}` — `selection_changed` is false on revision mismatch and true on selection-hash mismatch. **Neither CAS key** → unbound Catalog bulk remains compatible. **Successful bound Dismiss returns the existing selection-summary response shape** (`n`/`bytes`/`finalized`/…); it does **not** invent `ok: true`.
  5. **Post-dismiss re-preview.** Successful bound Dismiss is atomic, removes exact IDs, bumps revision once, and the UI automatically re-previews via `GET /api/plan/preview`. Replan is preview-only (never publish/approve/start Fill).
  6. **`_admission_terminal`.** Deleted in Gate 2 after zero-caller reconfirm. Do not revive as a second evidence authority.
- rationale: Gate 0 confirmed pure preview and bulk deselect already exist but the portal never exposes pure preview, and re-preview-then-bulk still races selection/revision changes. A dedicated read-only snapshot avoids holding the portal write lock across planning (sibling of `shadow_explain`). Atomic revision+selection-hash CAS is the smallest fail-closed binding that makes Dismiss authority-correct. Complete-pair fail-closed prevents half-bound requests from silently taking the unbound path. Reduced response keeps the full assignment payload off the browser while preserving the exact Q2 refusal container. Fill owns the notice because that is where plan admission blockers already surface; Catalog bulk stays the mutation primitive. Preserving the existing summary shape on success avoids a second, invented success envelope and keeps Catalog/Fill clients compatible.
- impact: Gate-1 contracts `tests/test_blocked_selection_gate1_contracts.py`; Gate-2 remediation contracts `tests/test_blocked_selection_gate2_remediation.py`; browser contracts in `tests/test_e2e_portal.py`. **INC-027** is allocated (baseline certificate self-copy; excluded from INC-023 by DEC-057). **DEF-035 remains unallocated.**
- 2026-07-30 UPDATE — Gate-1 remediation: successful bound removal preserves the existing selection-summary response (no `ok=True`); PREVIEW_STALE evidence distinguishes revision mismatch (`selection_changed: false`) from selection-hash mismatch (`selection_changed: true`); coherent read snapshot is BEGIN…end before close, not merely `connect(read_only=True)`.
- 2026-07-31 UPDATE — **Gate 2 accepted at `7db75ddacbbb472bdf9d61934ab8b30b87c42fd1`.** Production landed at `91c51d922e4472d43bce829ebfcc0e1dd223d588` (`plan_api.preview`, bulk CAS, Fill notice, `_admission_terminal` deletion). Gate-2 remediation at `7db75dd` adds complete-pair fail-closed (`PREVIEW_BINDINGS_REQUIRED`) and remediation contracts. **Frozen blocked-selection production tip: `7db75dd`** — no further blocked-selection production without new authorization. Evidence at freeze tip: Gate-1 contracts 16 passed; Gate-2 remediation contracts 6 passed; full non-E2E suite 668 passed; portal E2E all passed; `ruff check modelark scripts tests` clean; CI run 30600755870 success on Python 3.10, 3.12, and e2e.
- scope_boundary: Authority, concurrency, and response shape for blocked-selection. Freeze does not authorize INC-023, DEC-053/054, schema migration, selection cutover (DEF-CATALOG-004), prevention-at-finalize, or merge of PR #55.

### INC-027: Execution projection self-copies baseline certificates after any-row presence
- date: 2026-07-31 / status: accepted at `90c685077506e5679d82b1831c3058a4a9a5f1d7` / triggered_by: INC-023 Gate 0 inventory — baseline path in `project_pure` / `_catalog_projection_bundle` is not exact multi-file content evidence; excluded from INC-023 production by DEC-057 scope freeze / related: INC-023, DEC-049, DEC-053, DEC-054, DEC-057, DEC-060, RFC-002, modelark/execution_projection.py, modelark/execution_session.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: A baseline_satisfied requirement can pass projection certificate checks when any archived row for the repo remains on the satisfying drive, even if the multi-file planned set is incomplete or the certificate is only the proposal's stored value echoed back.
- root_cause: `execution_session._catalog_projection_bundle` (`modelark/execution_session.py:407-424`) selects **one** archive row (`SELECT orig_sha256 … LIMIT 1`) and sets `certificates[rid] = cert or row[0]`, returning the proposal's existing `baseline_certificate` whenever any row is present. `execution_projection.project_pure` baseline block (`modelark/execution_projection.py:238-281`) then accepts that certificate when `got_cert` matches, and treats archival presence as “any row for repo on drive” or the hardcoded key `(repo, "model.safetensors", label)` — not exact multi-file planned-set evidence.
- blast_radius: Baseline certificate revalidation at start/refresh is weaker than approve-time `_baseline_file_evidence` multi-file construction. Does not by itself fix executable multi-file shrink (INC-023). Predates the INC-023 fabrication defaults; under DEC-057 it must not expand INC-023 Gate production.
- why_not_caught_earlier: Attention focused on executable shrink and fill content satisfaction (DEC-055); baseline certificate path looked “present vs missing” only.
- planned_remediation: Disposition owed during the final **DEC-053 / DEC-054** provenance and baseline-evidence cycle — recompute exact multi-file baseline evidence without self-copying the proposal certificate on any-row presence. Not authorized as INC-023 Gate-2 production.
- scope_boundary: Baseline certificate self-copy and any-row / hardcoded-filename baseline archival check. Explicitly **out of INC-023 Gate-1/2 production**; INC-023 contracts may only document this boundary.
- 2026-08-04 UPDATE — **Gate 2 accepted / production frozen at `90c685077506e5679d82b1831c3058a4a9a5f1d7`.** Gate-1 contracts at `9109c6e` (after remediation); Gate-2 production at `90c6850` with no Gate-2 remediation required. **Frozen INC-027 production tip: `90c6850`** — no further INC-027 production without new authorization. Guarantees at that tip: (1) current baseline certificates are **recomputed independently per requirement_id** in `_catalog_projection_bundle`; (2) evidence uses `proposal._baseline_file_evidence` and the canonical constructor `proposal_canonical.baseline_satisfaction_certificate` (current catalog `full_manifest_hash`, satisfying drive, current identity epoch and fingerprint, complete archived-unfiltered file evidence); (3) stored proposal `baseline_certificate` values are **never echoed** as current evidence; (4) complete archive absence emits `"__MISSING__"` and refuses `APPROVAL_PROJECTION_VIOLATION` / `baseline_archive_missing`; (5) missing, null, absent, or unequal current certificates (or a missing stored certificate) refuse `APPROVAL_PROJECTION_VIOLATION` / `baseline_certificate_mismatch`; (6) same-epoch `identity_fingerprint` drift is caught through certificate mismatch; (7) epoch / lifecycle / manifest-drift precedence remains unchanged (`APPROVED_TARGET_IDENTITY_CHANGED`, `drive_lifecycle`, `APPROVED_INPUT_CHANGED` / `full_manifest_hash`); (8) any-row presence and the hardcoded `(repo, "model.safetensors", label)` baseline presence authority are **removed**. Evidence at freeze tip: `tests/test_inc027_gate1_contracts.py` 19 passed; full non-E2E suite 782 passed, 5 warnings; related session/projection tests 29 passed; ruff and `git diff --check` clean. **All planned product gates on this series branch are complete** (INC-023/024/025, blocked-selection, DEC-053/054, DEF-033, INC-027). Next operator-requested step is the **whole-PR Opus review** before merge — not further gate production, live migration, repair, Fill, drive work, or selection cutover by this closeout.

### DEC-059: Catalog migrations are staged on a verified clone before atomic publication
- date: 2026-07-31 / status: accepted / triggered_by: operator — the installation has one authoritative catalog and must be able to rehearse the DEC-053 provenance migration and its failure paths repeatedly without consuming the only rollback opportunity / related: DEC-014, DEC-024, DEC-050, DEC-052, DEC-053, DEC-054, DEF-034, RFC-001, RFC-002, modelark/core/db.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: A new schema migration never runs destructively against the operator's sole canonical catalog. (1) Capture a transactionally consistent source snapshot using SQLite's backup mechanism, or with every writer stopped and the database safely checkpointed; copying `catalog.sqlite` alone while WAL writers may exist is not an admissible snapshot. Record its schema version, integrity result, canonical row/content identity, and snapshot provenance. (2) Preserve that source unchanged and run migration, backfill, injected-failure tests, and post-migration validation on a disposable working clone. The same source may be cloned and exercised arbitrarily many times. (3) Validate at least SQLite integrity and foreign keys, the exact schema/version/constraints, row and content preservation, backfill classifications and disagreement counts, representative application open/planning/projection paths, and rollback under injected failure. (4) A failed rehearsal quarantines or discards only the clone: it never stamps, replaces, or partially mutates the canonical source. Every valid catalog state already admitted by the current release must have deterministic migration behaviour; contradictory evidence is never guessed and may stop clone validation for explicit disposition, but cannot strand the installation or destroy its usable source. (5) Publish only after the clone passes, all ModelArk writers are stopped, and the operator explicitly authorises cutover. Publication retains the original catalog as a recoverable rollback artifact and uses an atomic same-filesystem transition where supported. (6) `PRAGMA user_version` describes the published schema only; DEC-054 per-drive data-repair completion remains independent of the schema ladder.
- rationale: Backup-first in-place DDL still spends the one live migration attempt and can leave the operator choosing between a failed new file and manual restoration. Clone-first rehearsal turns migration into repeatable evidence: schema reconstruction, real-data classification, application compatibility, and fault paths can all fail safely before the publication boundary. The operational guarantee is not that inconsistent evidence is silently accepted; it is that discovery or implementation failure never removes the last known-usable catalog and that all catalog states the product already calls valid are covered before release.
- impact: The DEC-053/DEF-034 migration contracts must exercise a genuine frozen source clone and a separate publication/cutover seam, not only an in-place `db.connect()` ladder. Migration success on the acceptance fixture is necessary but not sufficient: rollback and repeatability are acceptance requirements, and the canonical runtime catalog remains untouched throughout rehearsal. Existing migration helpers may remain useful inside the clone; this decision changes where they run and what constitutes safe publication.
- 2026-08-02 UPDATE — **implementation accepted at production tip `0d723c1`.** Clone-first snapshot, disposable clone migration/rehearsal, validation, and atomic same-filesystem publication seams shipped under DEC-053/054 Gate 2. Live catalog cutover remains operator-authorized and has not been executed under this freeze.
- scope_boundary: Governs catalog snapshot, rehearsal, validation, publication, and rollback. It authorises no migration or repair against the live catalog, no automatic restore, no Fill, no Drive-02 write/probe, no PR merge, and no relaxation of DEC-053's disagreement refusal or DEC-054's per-drive repair rules.

### DEC-060: The provenance cycle separates schema publication, per-drive repair, and unresolved evidence
- date: 2026-08-01 / status: accepted / triggered_by: DEC-053/DEC-054 Gate-0 product disposition — migration, repair activation, replica healing, policy errors, and INC-027 needed explicit boundaries before contracts could be written / related: DEC-050, DEC-053, DEC-054, DEC-055, DEC-057, DEC-059, DEF-033, DEF-034, INC-027, RFC-001, RFC-002, modelark/core/db.py, modelark/hash_repair.py, modelark/verifier.py, modelark/execution_projection.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- decision: (1) **Activation.** One shared repair engine is invoked only by an explicit maintenance command and, optionally, by an operator-visible Fill preflight. Generic `db.connect()` and portal startup never begin repair; the portal may report pending or blocked state. (2) **`derivation_mode`.** The DEC-053/DEF-034 migration permits NULL for historical compatibility and constrains every non-null value to `optimized`, `state_truncated`, or `canonical_fallback`. It does not invent a value for an old proposal; every new proposal writer emits one of the named values. (3) **Replica healing.** Mirroring may fill a target's null digest and provenance from a compatible non-null source, or fill missing target provenance when source and target digests already match. Any non-null digest mismatch halts and surfaces. It never overwrites arbitrary non-null target metadata and propagates the source provenance verbatim rather than inventing a `mirrored` class. (4) **Repair state.** A dedicated `drive_hash_repair_state` row is keyed by `(drive_label, identity_epoch)` and retains the identity fingerprint as evidence. Its closed status vocabulary is `pending`, `running`, `blocked_absent`, `needs_refetch`, `halted`, and `complete`; `complete` means no unresolved repair candidate remains for that exact drive identity. State transitions and repaired archive rows commit atomically, so a partial run cannot claim completion, and a replacement identity never inherits its predecessor's completion. (5) **Cycle boundary.** INC-027 is completed in this overall provenance cycle but receives its own DEC-057 contract/production gate after the schema/provenance work; it is not folded invisibly into migration or repair. (6) **Verifier policy failure.** DEF-033 resolves to an operator-visible `unknown`: it is neither clean nor physical suspect and authorises no automatic repair, restore, deletion, or other destructive action.
- migration_shape: `archived.orig_sha256_provenance` is an additive nullable column with its CHECK enforced at creation on every declared supported SQLite runtime; rebuilding `archived` is not the default and requires concrete compatibility evidence. `placement_proposals` must be rebuilt to constrain its already-existing `derivation_mode` column while preserving NULL history. An orphaned `archived` row is an existing foreign-key/integrity failure that stops clone validation for explicit disposition; it is not silently classified as `legacy_unknown`. Gate-1 contracts must prove these shapes, the DEC-059 clone/publication seam, rollback, and supported-runtime behaviour rather than testing only automatic in-place migration on connect.
- state_disposition: An absent but not lost drive is `blocked_absent`. A tier-3 candidate is `needs_refetch` until the operator authorises and completes that path. A lost drive with archived facts follows the loss/integrity disposition and is not declared repair-complete merely because it cannot be mounted. Drive-02 has no archived facts, so its operator-declared loss creates no hash-repair obligation. Fingerprint mismatch or contradictory non-null digest evidence halts instead of guessing.
- rationale: Schema readiness, evidence repair, and physical-drive availability have different authorities and failure modes. Keeping them separate lets a clone prove migration safety without touching media, lets an absent drive remain ordinary unfinished work, and prevents either a schema version or a portal open from laundering incomplete evidence into a completed state. The nullable historical field preserves truth better than a fabricated classification, while the closed non-null vocabulary prevents new ambiguity.
- scope_boundary: Binds product semantics and the remaining gate order. It authorises Gate-1 contract design after the corrected Gate-0 inventory is accepted, but authorises no production implementation, migration or repair against the live catalog, refetch, Fill run, Drive-02 probe/write, selection cutover, merge, or automatic publication of a rehearsed clone.

### DEC-053 / DEC-054 production freeze (Gate 2 accepted)
- date: 2026-08-02 / status: accepted / triggered_by: reviewer acceptance of DEC-053/054 Gate 2 at `0d723c1028edd100feb6c5b815a842c8f2b5691a` / related: DEC-053, DEC-054, DEC-059, DEC-060, DEF-033, DEF-034, INC-027, modelark/core/db.py, modelark/hash_repair.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md

### INC-028: Content-satisfaction consumers bypass DEC-055 and falsely complete approved work
- date: 2026-08-04 / status: active — Gate 0 recorded; Gate 1 contracts authorized / triggered_by: independent whole-PR review of PR #55 at `c21bd787ef4c1f654e1c9052dc8d4dfd33e9ae5d` / related: DEC-019, DEC-049, DEC-051, DEC-054, DEC-055, DEC-060, INC-023, INC-024, INC-025, INC-027, modelark/archive_hash.py, modelark/proposal.py, modelark/fill.py, modelark/fetch.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md, tests/test_inc028_gate1_contracts.py
- symptom: Three consumers still conclude that approved content needs no work from archive-row presence or file size instead of the DEC-055 digest rule. `fetch_model` can discard an explicit approved missing manifest and let Fill report `PLAN_SATISFIED` after transferring zero bytes; proposal baseline classification can accept a hashless, unresolvable copy on size alone; and the replica drain can mark every co-batched requirement complete after one copied file or spin forever after a zero-progress batch.
- authority: One pure shared content-satisfaction predicate consumes only supplied frozen approved digest and archived evidence and resolves through `archive_hash.expected_sha256`; it accepts no connection and performs no catalog, wishlist, or acquisition-policy lookup. Caller-specific size checks remain additional constraints. Approved execution must not reopen the live policy/file authority closed by INC-025.
- disposition: A missing target row is ordinary acquisition work. A present target with a non-null resolved digest different from the approved digest is `APPROVED_TARGET_DIGEST_MISMATCH`; it is preserved and requires operator inspection/disposition before refetch. A present target whose original-byte digest cannot be resolved is `APPROVED_TARGET_DIGEST_UNPROVABLE`; DEC-054 repair is the first action, not immediate refetch. Both are decided before download and carry repository, filename, drive, approved digest, resolved digest, and archived evidence. Neither reuses the post-download `TargetPathConflictError` type. Replica healing follows DEC-060: compatible null evidence may be filled with source digest/provenance verbatim; contradictory non-null digests halt before copy, metadata mutation, or upsert.
- completion_rule: `stored_repos` and aggregate `copied_files` are progress signals, never satisfaction. FETCH and REPLICATE requirements complete only after content satisfaction is re-derived from current durable facts against their exact frozen approved file rows. Per-requirement/file outcomes prevent batch-wide completion, and zero-progress work consumes a bounded attempt budget and terminalizes rather than looping.
- scope_boundary: Gate 1 is contracts only. No production implementation, live catalog query, migration, repair, Fill, drive operation, selection mutation, PR state change, or merge is authorized by this entry.

### INC-029: Clone-first migration mishandles SQLite sidecars and self-validates disagreement evidence
- date: 2026-08-04 / status: active — recorded for a later gate / triggered_by: independent whole-PR review of PR #55 at `c21bd78` / related: INC-012, DEC-053, DEC-059, modelark/core/db.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md
- symptom: Publication rejects an existing `catalog.sqlite` but not destination `-wal`, `-shm`, or `-journal` files, so a foreign WAL can replay over the validated replacement. Rehearsal opens the canonical source read-write and can checkpoint/delete a stopped-writer hot WAL, contradicting source immutability. The successful report also hardcodes a zero disagreement count instead of threading the measured classification result.
- boundary: Requires its own migration Gate 0/1 design. Rehearsal source immutability and publication-time canonical-source quiescence are distinct constraints; redirecting the publication `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` lock to a copy is not authorized. No migration production or live rehearsal/publication is authorized here.

### INC-030: The explicit per-drive repair command omits its archive resolver
- date: 2026-08-04 / status: active — recorded for a later gate / triggered_by: independent whole-PR review of PR #55 at `c21bd78` / related: DEC-054, DEC-060, modelark/cli.py, modelark/hash_repair.py / docs_updated: docs/decision_log.md

### INC-031: Bound Dismiss treats non-refused HTTP error bodies as success
